# Changelog

All notable changes to this crate will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed

- `codegen_c::from_program`: a closure body's captured values take the kinds every `MakeClosure` that builds it stores
  (`capture_kinds`, settled with the parameters), so its arithmetic and its result agree with what its callers read; a
  popped value is the list's element (no `[present, value]` box), as `resolve_pop` guarantees an item to every pop.
- The collector prints the heap report a program asks for even when it never allocated (`lc_gc_report_unstarted`).
- `codegen_riscv::RISCV_RUNTIME_C` carries no call of the C library's allocator: the libc fallback moved out of
  `lc_gc_api_source` into `lc_gc_libc_source`, which only the C runtime emits, and the RISC-V runtime refuses to compile
  where the collector cannot run.

### Fixed

- The optimizer's linear prover (`optimize::affine::{consistent, prove}`) eliminates under a budget of 4,096
  constraints a step (`lia::fourier_motzkin_unsat_within`): a system past it counts as satisfiable and its goal as
  not proven, so loop-interleaved recursion the inliner unfolds compiles (`largo run` never finished on it).

## [0.11.0] - 2026-11-02

### Added

- `readReals(path)`: the `Seq of Real` a file of little-endian binary64 values holds, eight bytes a value in order —
  the numbers a program reads at run time rather than carries as code, such as a model's weights. The same bits on the
  tree-walker, the VM and its native tier, C (both backends), Rust, RISC-V and WebAssembly (the node shim reads the
  file; a page in the browser refuses by name); a file it cannot read, or one that is not a whole number of values,
  stops the program in `logicaffeine_system::file::read_reals`'s words. `BuiltinId::ReadReals`, wire id appended.
- `codegen::absent`: the Rust storage of what a stored `nothing` reaches (`LogosOpt` at the depths
  `analysis::absence` gives), operations on a value-or-nothing in the VM's words, and changes to a place written
  level by level with copy-on-write at each shared list or map.
- `analysis::absence`: where a stored `nothing` can be, as depths of each binding, parameter, result and field;
  `codegen_c::absent` (value-or-nothing storage at those depths, the `Seq_logos_opt` and `Map_{str,i64}_logos_opt`
  families) and `codegen_c::equality` (equality of C values as the VM compares them, the exact order of an integer
  and a real); `optimize::facts::Fact::Vacated`.
- `codegen_sva::hart` (`model`, `rvc`, `soc`, `image`, `ddr2`, `rtl`: the hart's model, its RTL and SoC files and the
  boot image format) and `codegen_sva::onboard` (the compiler as the board runs it: the library, `compile`, `report`);
  examples `onboard_compile` and `onboard_image`.
- `accel::region` (`discover`, `Discovered`: the device regions of an optimized program, each a CFG of checked cells)
  and `accel::c_model` (`model_c`: the generated C device model); `compile::compile_to_program_optimized_named`.
- `codegen_sva::timing_graph::Delays::STRATIX10` and `STRATIX10_NS_PER_LEVEL`.
- `spatial::decode_engine::machine::{Fold, fold}` and `Machine::folded`: a residual add or a gated product folded into
  the matrix before it (`Job::Matvec { fold }`, the fold's code in the job word); `StoreLayout` laid at the step's
  widest point (`StoreLayout::widest`, `STORE_ORDERS`). `rtl::engine::Streaming::with_folds` and `logos_fold_lane_p`;
  `capability::Gap::Folds`.
- `spatial::serving::StateFlush::Trailing { tokens }`; `spatial::decode_engine::model::{Replay, TRAILING_TOKENS}`;
  `numerics::gdn_fold_trailing`; `rtl::engine::{logged, behind, recurrence_log}`, `Streaming::signature` and
  `EngineParams::streaming_signature`; `capability::Gap::Recurrence { has, needs }`.
- `spatial::decode_engine::machine::{DeltaNorm, fold_delta_norms, gated_norm_job}`, `Job::Delta { norm }` and
  `Machine::delta_normed`; `rtl::engine::Streaming::{with_delta_norm, with_recurrence_credit, with_recurrence_flushes}`;
  `rtl::gdn::{LANE_CREDIT_MAX, FOLD_M_READ_LATENCY}`, `logos_gdn_gate_p` and `logos_gdn_rows`.
- `spatial::decode_engine::vector::Op::{MulAdd, MulAddScalar}`, on which `vector::conv` runs;
  `rtl::engine::{VECTOR_PASSES_MAX, PORT_PIPES}`; `Streaming::{with_conv_path, conv_beats}` and `logos_conv_path`.
- `spatial::decode_engine::rtl::column::ColumnSet::{with_token_pairs, issue_rows, issues}` and
  `matvec_pass_x2t_verilog`; `ofs::AfuConfig::token_pairs`.
- `spatial::decode_engine::rtl::ram::{MLAB_WORDS, RAMSTYLE, STORE_READ_LATENCY, STORE_GROUP, store_verilog}` and
  `rtl::simd::SIMD_P_READ_LATENCY`.
- `spatial::decode_engine::ofs::{job_listing, job_listing_of, MARK_LEAD, ENGINE_SETTINGS, compute_fifo_sides,
  FIFO_SIDE_REGISTERS}`; `logos_compute_clock` takes `LEAD`.
- `codegen_sva::timing_graph::{Driver, Fanout, Wide, memory_blocks}` and `TimingGraph::{fanouts, wide}`.
- `examples/d5005_afu.rs`: `--folds`, `--behind`, `--recurrence-behind`, `--recurrence-flushes`, `--recurrence-credit`,
  `--delta-norm`, `--conv-path`, `--token-pairs` and `--job-listing`.
- `spatial::decode_engine::rtl::engine::CONV_PATH_VREGS` and `Streaming::with_vector_depth`; `examples/d5005_afu.rs`:
  `--vector-depth`.
- `codegen_sva::terminal_protocol` (the Fabric Terminal's link frames, screen geometry, palette, activity and totals
  fields, `table_json` for hosts), `codegen_sva::terminal_rtl` (the terminal's modules, tops and `board_files`, the
  font ROM and its license, `render` of a screen) and `codegen_sva::engine_build` (the fabric engine's lifters,
  `lower`/`lower_main`, `fits_caps`, the VM golden and `fabric_map`, lifted from `emit_wpe_soc`);
  `wprog_engine_rtl::emit_wprog_engine_soc_verilog_hw_probed` (the deployed engine with `probe_*` observer ports;
  without them the engine is byte-identical); example `fabric_terminal` (`--table`, `--emit DIR`).
- `model::hf` (`HfCheckpoint::open`/`open_with`, `Recompute`, `tokenizer_meta`, `recomputed`, `unread`, `skipped`,
  `shard_count`; `HfTokenizer`, `HfPreTokenizer`, `nfc`; `PRE_TOKENIZER_DIGESTS`, `PRE_TOKENIZER_PROBE`,
  `PATTERN_VERSION`, `NORMAL_FORM_VERSION`, `pre_tokenizer_named`), `model::safetensors` (`Shard`, `Entry`, `Dtype`, `shard_paths`),
  `quant::f32_to_bf16`, and `tokenizer::Vocab::from_meta`, which `from_gguf` now calls.
- `model::bind`: `View::declared` (the layout a view reads a tensor into), `q8_planes`/`Q8Planes` (a Q8_0 weight's
  int8 values and block scales in its declared layout, repacked from the checkpoint's blocks: what a bundle manifest
  names `q8_0.values` and `q8_0.scales`), and `rotary_values` (a rotary table's values from the spec and the
  checkpoint's frequency factors).
- `spatial::streamed` (was `qwen38_streamed`): any `ModelSpec` lowers as one program per layer kind — `layer_kinds`
  (by the digest of each layer's program up to its names and storage), `renaming`, `Storage::{AllQ8, Checkpoint}`,
  `lower_{forward,decode,tp_decode,batched_decode,prefill,batched_tp_decode}_streamed`; `rebind` leaves a rotary table
  (`Rcos1`) every layer's. `compile::compile_model_to_rdu_streamed`, `RduModel` (`qwen38`, `from_checkpoint`) and
  `StepShape` replace `compile_qwen38_to_rdu_streamed` and `QwenDecode`; the manifest's model names its architecture.
  `model::Checkpoint` and `model::open_checkpoint` (a GGUF file or a Hugging Face directory); `model::bind`'s
  `stored_as_q8_planes` and `View::keeps_blocks_down_rows`; `spatial::fit::admits_tp` for any spec.
- `spatial::rdu_chip`: every published fact cites a pinned bibliography key; `pmu_kib`, `hbm_bandwidth_gbps`,
  `ddr_bandwidth_gbps`, `p2p_tb_s`, `others` (`OtherFigure`), `BINARY`; `cites` are bibliography keys. The SN40L's HBM
  roof is 1.6 TB/s and its peak 638 TFLOPS (its node 10.208 PFLOPS, derived); the SN50's HBM and DDR rates are modeled.
- A softmax at a precision other than the target's element precision names its scaler by it (`sm_scaler_fp32`), so an
  expert router's beside an attention's composes. `model::safetensors` reads by position on every target.
- The spatial IR lowers a windowed attention (`within a window of W`) in both forms: the sections' mask keeps the last
  `W` positions below each row's limit, the flash fold's blocks mask below their limit less `W`. An attention with
  sinks (`with sinks S`) folds each head's sink into its softmax's maximum and denominator; the flash fold starts each
  row's maximum at its sink and its sum at one, the sinks spread one tile per item.
- The spatial IR lowers gpt-oss's and DeepSeek-V2's mixtures: the router's bias, the experts' gate, up and down
  biases, the clamped SwiGLU and the weighting without renormalizing, in the flat and the blocked form (the blocked
  form gathers the chosen expert's bias tiles by its pick and mixes the down biases by the router weights).
- The spatial IR lowers a latent attention's decode at whole-tile widths: a head slice and a join move tiles a block at
  a time, a per-head matmul is a GEMV per head that reads only its head's rows of one tile column, and the flash fold
  takes values narrower than its keys, folding them an accumulator page at a time; a decode fold too large for a site is
  one fold per value page, joined per head. DeepSeek-V2-Lite's step (16 heads, keys 576, values 512) lowers whole and
  certifies on the SN50. A flat join, head slice or row write wider than an accumulator page runs an item per block of
  its result (`FlatBuild::columns`).
- `spatial::handoff` (`contract`, `Contract::{check, take, of_layer, to_json}`, `Carried`, `Handoff`,
  `HandoffError`): the state a decode step carries — every attention layer's cache, every gated-delta layer's memory and
  conv rings — named and shaped as the author declares it, so a prompt prefilled on one chip decodes on another and a
  model's layers split over chips hand their hidden state on. Every decode bundle's manifest states it (`handoff`).
- `model::triage` (`triage`, `refusal`, `tensors_read`, `Reading`, `ARCHITECTURES`): a checkpoint whose architecture
  name no arm reads is read under every architecture an arm reads; a reading whose decode step reads exactly the
  checkpoint's tensors names a family it carries under another name, else the nearest reading is named with the tensors
  it lacks and leaves unread, after it every reading that does not read the checkpoint at all. `largo emit rdu --model`
  refuses such a checkpoint with it.
- `spatial::tensor_parallel::TensorParallelShim`: a chip's shim for a tensor-parallel program — the chip's own answer,
  less any fused op over two chips, a chip and the replicated spine, or a collective's fold. The program walk optimizes a
  tensor-parallel program under it.
- Llama 4 as a spec: `model::spec::{Window, Temperature}`, `Attention::{temperature, qk_unit_norm}` and
  `Gating::SigmoidScaledInput`; the spec's `llama4` arm (three rotated layers in chunks of 8192 positions with a
  weightless QK norm after the rotation, then a rotation-free layer whose queries take the attention temperature; a
  mixture every `interleave_moe_layer_step`-th layer scored by sigmoids, one expert's input scaled by its weight, beside
  a shared expert). The f64 reference computes all of it. The author writes it: the weightless norm as an RMSNorm whose
  weight is a host row of ones, the temperature as a rotation whose cosine is the position's factor and whose sine is
  zero, the routing as `moe … by sigmoid without renormalizing weighting the inputs`; a temperature over an odd head
  width is refused.
- `model::bind::{HostTable, host_values, computed_values, computed_tables, temperature}`: the tables a host computes
  from the spec rather than reads from the checkpoint — the rotary tables, a row of ones, the temperature's rotation —
  named and filled through one entry point. An RDU decode bundle writes every one its programs read; a streamed bundle
  keeps their names from layer to layer.
- `tensor_recognize::qwen::{Window, MoeScore}`; `MoeSpec::{score, weigh_inputs}`: a mixture scored by its logits'
  sigmoids, and one whose kept weights scale its experts' inputs, lifted from their desugars. The spatial IR lowers both
  in the flat and the blocked forms — a NaN score is never routed ahead of a number, as the desugar's scan never picks
  one — and refuses renormalized weights that scale the inputs by name; the tensor-graph evaluator computes both; the
  CUDA forward and lane refuse them by name. The spatial IR keeps a chunked-local attention's own chunk in the sections
  and the flash forms; the lane refuses a chunk by name.
- `model::spec` reads Qwen 2.5 (`qwen2`: biased projections under a NEOX rotation) and Mixtral (a `llama` checkpoint
  that counts experts: a softmax router whose top-k weights are renormalized, each expert as wide as
  `expert_feed_forward_length` or else the FFN); `model::hf` reads `Qwen2ForCausalLM`, `MistralForCausalLM` and
  `MixtralForCausalLM` directories as the converter writes them (Mixtral's `block_sparse_moe` experts stacked and its
  router read), and refuses a Mistral window the llama GGUF cannot carry by name.
- DeepSeek-V3's routing: `tensor_recognize::qwen::{MoeGroups}` and `MoeSpec::{selection_bias, groups, scale}` lift
  `moe … biased by B`, `within the top G of N groups` and `scaled by S`; `MoeSpec::weighs_outputs_by_softmax` names
  the router every other lowering knows and `MoeSpec::sum_can_clamp` where a renormalizing sum can fall below 2⁻¹⁴.
  Both spatial forms of the mixture choose by the selection scores among the top groups' experts (each group scored by
  its two best), clamp the renormalizing sum where it can bind and scale the kept weights; the tensor graph evaluates
  them as the desugar does; the CUDA forward and lane refuse every router past a softmax weighting its experts'
  outputs by name. `model::author` writes `Gating::Sigmoid { bias: true }`, a group limit and a routed scale, the
  selection bias bound to `blk.N.exp_probs_b.bias`; a group limit over a softmax of the kept logits stays refused.
- Qwen's gated shared experts: `model::spec::Moe::shared_gate`, and the `qwen2moe` arm of `ModelSpec::from_source`
  (llama.cpp's `models/qwen2moe.cpp`: an expert `feed_forward_length` over the experts a token takes and the shared expert
  `feed_forward_length` when their widths are unnamed or 0, block 0's of a per-block width array, no routed scale). The
  reference scales the shared experts by `σ(x·g)`, `g` the checkpoint's `ffn_gate_inp_shexp`; `model::author` writes it
  (a one-column product, its sigmoid spread across the row by the host's `Ones(d)`, which `bind::computed_tables` lists).
  `model::hf` reads `Qwen2MoeForCausalLM`, refuses `norm_topk_prob` where llama.cpp's graph fixes it the other way
  (Qwen2-MoE never renormalizes, Qwen3-MoE always), and reads a SentencePiece model only for Qwen2 and Qwen3, as the
  converter's classes do. The llama, qwen3moe, gpt-oss and llama4 arms leave `expert_weights_scale` unread, as llama.cpp
  does; `model::triage::ARCHITECTURES` names every arm. The f64 reference ranks a NaN router score below every number, as
  the chip's routing does, where it panicked comparing one.
- Qwen3.5-MoE: the `qwen35moe` arm of `ModelSpec::from_source` (llama.cpp `models/qwen35moe.cpp`), Qwen's mixture one
  builder both Qwen arms read; `model::hf` reads `Qwen3_5MoeForConditionalGeneration` and `Qwen3_5MoeForCausalLM` by
  Qwen3.5's hybrid rules and Qwen's mixture's, and splits a Qwen mixture's stored `mlp.experts.gate_up_proj` (the gate's
  rows first) as the converter does.
- Qwen3-Next: `model::layout` presents a checkpoint in the layout the spec's arms read (a Qwen3-Next checkpoint's
  value-head axes reordered from grouped by key head to tiled, its `ssm_ba` split into `ssm_beta` and `ssm_alpha`), and
  `model::open_checkpoint` opens every checkpoint through it. The `qwen3next` arm of `ModelSpec::from_source` reads
  Qwen3.5-MoE's blocks and refuses a checkpoint still holding `ssm_ba`, by name. `model::hf` reads `Qwen3NextForCausalLM`
  as the converter writes it: `in_proj_qkvz` split per key head into `attn_qkv` and `attn_gate`, `in_proj_ba` whole as
  `ssm_ba`, its post-attention norm as `post_attention_norm`, its draft blocks counted from `mtp.layers` where the
  configuration names no count. `model::triage::ARCHITECTURES` names `qwen3next`.
- `model::author` writes an attention whose score scale is not 1/√hd with the sugar's `scaled by S` clause after its
  window clause, where `check` refused it.
- `spatial`: a blocked mixture whose router row is wider than an accumulator page (more than 256 experts on the SN50)
  scores and routes it in blocks of the row (`wide_softmax`, `wide_sigmoid`, `wide_router_bias`, `wide_moe_routing`:
  each block's top K as candidates, then the top K of them), and one with more picks than a page holds stores its pick
  and weight tables and sums its rounds in pages; `moe_blocks` takes both, and refuses a group limit over a wide row.
- `model::hf`: Gated DeltaNet's gate activation (`output_gate_type`, Qwen3.8's configurations) is read: SiLU, swish by
  its other name, as the spec holds it; any other activation is refused naming it. `language_model_only` (which towers
  a server loads) is inert.
- `model::spec`: `ModelSpec::{hyper, ple, indexer}` and `GatedDelta::gate` carry Qwen3.8-Flash-Next's parts
  (`HyperConnections`: lanes and the mix's rank; `Ple`: the n-gram embedding's layer, orders, heads, row, convolution,
  end of document and hash constants; `Indexer`: QSA's heads, head width, budget, block and the layers that select;
  `GateActivation`: SiLU or the sigmoid). The `qwen4exp` arm reads them as llama.cpp does: QSA on the attention
  layers whose compression ratio is not zero, one ratio for them all; one n-gram layer, a Gated DeltaNet one.
- `model::hf`: Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (`Qwen4ExpForConditionalGeneration`, `Qwen4ExpForCausalLM`) read as llama.cpp's
  converter writes `qwen4exp`: its keys, every hyper-connection, index and n-gram tensor (the index projection split
  into queries and key, the zero-centred norms offset by one), the n-gram table's shards presented as one table and
  read a row at a time, its hash constants from the checkpoint's int64 tensors, and the draft block (`eh_proj` its
  two projections side by side: `Form::Beside`). Every layer transformers names `full_attention` or
  `indexed_attention` selects with QSA, as transformers computes it; the converter, reading the configuration
  through transformers, matches `full_attention` alone and writes those layers a ratio of 0. Refused by name: a gate
  other than the sigmoid llama.cpp fixes, or none stated; index key heads other than one; more than one n-gram layer;
  a draft-layer count or a convolution-state count that disagrees with the rest.
- `model::reference`: hyper-connection lanes (each sublayer's input mixed from the lanes, its output injected back,
  the head reading the last mix), the per-layer n-gram embedding (the hash in two's-complement 64-bit arithmetic, no
  n-gram reaching back across an end of document, the lane-gated value and its dilated convolution, both carried from
  step to step), QSA's selection of the attended positions (blocks of index keys averaged, normed and rotated at their
  first position, the best `budget / block` kept beside the positions after the last complete block), and Gated
  DeltaNet's gate by its activation.
- `model::author::check` refuses hyper-connections, the per-layer embedding, QSA and a Gated DeltaNet gated other than
  by SiLU by name, and `compile_model_to_rdu_streamed` checks the author before it lowers, so no bundle of a model the
  language does not write computes another one.
- `model::hf`: a checkpoint's class decides where its decoder's tensors are (`decoder_prefix`): Qwen3.5's multimodal
  checkpoints under `model.language_model.`, a Qwen3.5 text model alone (`Qwen3_5ForCausalLM`,
  `Qwen3_5MoeForCausalLM`: Qwen3.8-2.4T's layout) under `model.`, as every other class.
- `model::hf::HfCheckpoint::{read_configuration, read_tokenizer}`: a published configuration and tokenizer read without
  their weights. The reader reads or refuses the keys published configurations carry: `auto_map`, training settings,
  transformers 4's serialized `PretrainedConfig` attributes (`pruned_heads`, `add_cross_attention` and
  `is_encoder_decoder` refused unless at their defaults), DeepSeek's `moe_layer_freq` (refused unless 1), gpt-oss's
  `experts_per_token` and `initial_context_length` (held to the keys the converter reads), Qwen3-Next's
  `use_sliding_window`.
- `model::hf::Tiktoken` and `model::hf::tiktoken_split`: a tiktoken vocabulary (Kimi K2's) read and encoded as its
  tokenizer does, its pattern read out of its tokenizer code's text, never run; a DeepSeek directory with a
  `tiktoken.model` and no `tokenizer.json` reads as the converter's Kimi path writes it. The pattern engine reads a
  `tiktoken` pattern in `tiktoken`'s syntax: the Han script (`HAN`, Unicode 16.0's Scripts.txt), nested classes and
  `&&`; the tokenizers syntax refuses them as before.
- `model::quant::ScaleGrid` and `model::quant::{e4m3fn_to_f32, e5m2_to_f32}`: a scaled-float weight's scale broadcast
  as the converter's `dequant_simple` broadcasts it. `model::hf` reads an FP8 directory (`quant_method` fp8) in place,
  each weight beside its `_scale_inv` (or Mistral's `qscale_weight`) read times its scale under every view, the scales
  and activation scales consumed, and refuses every other quantized format by name.
- The `glm4moe` arm of `ModelSpec::from_source` (llama.cpp `models/glm4-moe.cpp`), which shares DeepSeek's mixture
  with the `deepseek2` arm; `model::layout` presents a `glm4moe` checkpoint's `post_attention_norm` as `ffn_norm`, and
  the arm refuses one read raw; `model::hf` reads `Glm4MoeForCausalLM` as the converter writes it, its draft block
  presented as `blk.<n>.nextn.*`. The vocabulary of a `glm4` or `glm4moe` directory takes the special tokens its
  converter names by text. `model::hf` reads Phi-3's and GLM's rotation share, and Phi-3's original context, from the
  rope parameters before the configuration's own key, and refuses a Phi-3 configuration naming no original context.
- The `glm4` arm of `ModelSpec::from_source` (llama.cpp `models/glm4.cpp`); `model::layout` presents a `glm4`
  checkpoint's fused `ffn_up` as `ffn_gate` and `ffn_up`, and the arm refuses one read raw and GLM-4V's multimodal
  rotation by name; `model::hf` reads `Glm4ForCausalLM` as the converter writes it.
- The `phi3` arm of `ModelSpec::from_source` (llama.cpp `models/phi3.cpp`); `model::layout` presents a `phi3`
  checkpoint's fused `ffn_up` as `ffn_gate` and `ffn_up`, and the arm refuses one read raw and LongRoPE's factor tables
  by name; `model::hf` reads `Phi3ForCausalLM` as the converter writes it.
- `model::spec::Attention::qk_norm_whole` and the `minimax-m2` arm of `ModelSpec::from_source` (llama.cpp
  `models/minimax-m2.cpp`); `model::hf` reads `MiniMaxM2ForCausalLM` as the converter writes it and refuses MiniMax-01's
  linear-attention layers, scaled residuals and shared expert by name; `model::reference` and `model::author` norm
  across every head where the model does, and `author::check_tp` refuses it over chips; the CUDA lane's `lane_check`
  and the D5005 engine refuse it by name.
- `model::author` writes an attention's `capped at C` after its scale, the logits `capped at C` where the model caps
  them (the product bound first as `<logits>_raw`), values `value_dim` wide (`Wv`, the value cache, the attended rows,
  `Wo`'s rows, and each chip's share of each when split), and a fused projection as its three projections. `check`
  refuses only an output gate on a fused projection or over values unlike the keys in width, as the reference does.
  `model::bind::View::FusedPart` reads one part of `attn_qkv` (its rows, transposed) or of its bias (its elements), each
  head's rotated dimensions reordered for an interleaved rotation; `codegen_cuda::lane::weights` places its rows. The CUDA
  lane refuses soft caps, a fused projection and values unlike the keys in width by name (`lane_check`), as it did
  while the author refused them.
- `tensor_recognize::qwen`: `AttentionSpec::softcap` and `SoftcapSpec` (`recognize_softcap`), an attention's
  `capped at C` and `Let Y be X capped at C`, each read only in the sugar's exact form. `tensor_graph::front`:
  `TensorOpSpec::Softcap`, lifted before the elementwise recognizer, its class given its exemplar in
  `tensor_graph::symbols`; `tensor_graph::eval` and `tensor_graph::vjp` compute both caps as the language does.
  `spatial::passes::from_qwen_ops::from_softcap`, and the cap in the attention's sections (each
  scaled score before the mask) and flash fold (each block's scores before its maximum); `spatial::shim` answers
  `softcap` native and `spatial::link` links a capped tensor whole. `codegen_cuda::{forward_shim, lane_shim, lane_link}`
  and `spatial::decode_engine::shim` refuse a capped attention and a capped tensor by name.
- `tensor_recognize::qwen::GateAct` and `GatedDeltaSpec::gate`: the gated delta's gate, `silu(z)` or `σ(z)`, read from
  the desugared form; `tensor_graph::eval` and `spatial::passes::from_qwen_ops` (its flat, blocked and chunked forms)
  compute it; `codegen_cuda::{lane_link, lane_shim, forward_shim}` and `spatial::decode_engine::capability` refuse the
  sigmoid by name; `model::author` writes `GatedDelta::gate` `Sigmoid` as `with sigmoid gate`.
- `model::hf`: `Gemma3ForConditionalGeneration` and `Llama4ForConditionalGeneration` read as their text models: the
  decoder found under `model.language_model.` or `language_model.model.` (`decoder_prefix` reads the stored names),
  the head beside it, the projector skipped with the tower; the wrappers' image keys inert; Llama 4's
  `for_llm_compressor` read, true (experts stored one linear each) refused by name.
- `model::spec`: the `gemma` and `gemma2` arms (llama.cpp `models/gemma.cpp` and `models/gemma2.cpp`); Gemma 2's caps
  50 and 30 where its file writes none, its score scale fixed by the stack's depth; one reader of
  `attention.sliding_window_pattern`, as flags or as a period, for Gemma 2 and 3. `model::hf`: `GemmaForCausalLM` and
  `Gemma2ForCausalLM` read as their converters write them (every norm an offset from one, a stored head dropped);
  refused by name: a `query_pre_attn_scalar` other than the scale llama.cpp fixes (Gemma 2 and 3), an unset Gemma 2
  cap, a second window size, another alternation of windows, `use_bidirectional_attention` (every Gemma); Gemma 2's
  rotation base written as its configuration states it; `unsloth_version` inert.
- `spatial::passes::from_qwen_ops`: the blocked gated delta holds one page of each buffer an item writes once (each
  a-block's store, the norm weight, the gated output) where two do not fit a site, as a real-width head's do at fp32;
  two otherwise. A decode attention's regroup of a K/V head's query heads and a flash fold's key, value and output pages take the
  same rule (`paged`).
- `spatial::passes::from_qwen_ops`: a gated delta whose heads are not whole tiles and whose memory is past one
  accumulator page of a row (the flat form holds the whole memory as one row: Qwen 3.5's four heads of 16 are 32
  tiles against the SN50's 8) is widened (`widened_gated_delta`): its operands and memory moved into whole-tile heads by sections that each load only
  the window of source tiles an output tile reads (`moved_runs`), the blocked step run at the widened width reading the
  head's own width for `1/√d` and the norm, each version of the widened memory named once, and the output and memory
  moved back to the program's layout. The blocked and chunked forms take that width and a name for each version of
  the memory; whole-tile heads lower as before.
- `spatial::passes::compose` holds a tensor only readers declare, at two widths of one float, at the wider, the
  narrower reader rounding it on the way in as below a wider producer; two producers, or a float against an integer,
  still refuse by name.
- The spatial IR rotates a head per item by a table row that is not whole tiles (a partial rotation's rows of 12 or
  40), reading the row through a window of whole tiles; a rotation whose heads are not whole tiles (heads of 80) is the
  one flat stage, blocked by its result's columns. A conv ring is a matrix whenever the single step's flat form does
  not fit a site. An elementwise op whose right operand is one row combined with every row (a bias over a prefill
  block) lowers in the dense and the flat layouts.
- `spatial::ir`: a section that stores its result in parts — several stores of it at one site and item — is covered by
  the tiles each store addresses, and `place` keeps each part's shape.
- `model::ngram_hash` (`NgramHash::new`/`from_config`, `multipliers`, `head_primes`, `head_offsets`,
  `rows_before_padding`, `rows`, `ids`): the row ids of Qwen3.8-Flash-Next's hashed n-gram embedding.
- `spatial::wide_int` (`Op64`, `LaneOp`, `LaneProgram`, `lower`, `prove`, `Proof`, `ProofKind`, `KernelVerdict`,
  `Identity`, `needs_limbs`): 64-bit add, subtract, multiply, bitwise operations, shifts and the remainder by a divisor of 32 bits
  or fewer as programs over 32-bit lanes, each with a proof checked afresh.
- `codegen_sva::timing_graph`: `TimingGraph::fanouts` and `TimingGraph::wide` (each net's loads, memory ports as the
  blocks they reach by `memory_blocks`, a register's `maxfan` as its copies, clocks left out, each net's `Driver`), from
  the `Fanout` a graph read from a netlist carries.
- `spatial::decode_engine::ofs::compute_fifo_sides` and `FIFO_SIDE_REGISTERS`: the shell's FIFO sides that step at the
  compute clock's step, read from the shell's connections, and the registers of a side.
- The one IR and its shared optimizer (`tensor_graph`; `benchmarks/FUSIONS_WE_NEED.md`, IR-0 to OPT-3):
  - `tensor_graph::front` (the front end, moved from `spatial::passes::recognize_stages`, which re-exports it),
    `graph` (`TensorGraph`, `Op`, `Sym`, `Access`, `Dst`; `Op::result`), `facts` (`Slots`, the dataflow index the
    rules read), `eval` (the evaluator, equal to the VM), `symbols` (`ClassId` and `CLASSES`, every class the book
    names), `shim` (`Shim`, `Support`, `Allow`, `allows`, `ReferenceShim`, `Linking`), `link` (`resolve`, `Linked`,
    `Unresolved`), `fuse` (the rule table, `optimize`, `realizes`, `Report`, `Decision`, `Outcome`) and `numerics`
    (quantize insertion and epilogue folds).
  - The shared table holds the lane's rules and every fused class the book witnesses: norm, GEMM and input epilogues,
    elementwise chains and regions, recompute fan-out, the GDN, MoE and MLA blocks, the gated attention's split,
    horizontal norm-rope-insert, a chunk's last-layer rows, and the attention and delta-rule output quantize. A rule
    builds a class only where the chip's shim links it whole.
  - `codegen_cuda::lane_shim::LaneShim` (the lane's answers, its kernel conditions and its own rules, `latent_expanded`
    and `gemv_parts`), `codegen_cuda::lane_link` (the lane's link from the optimized graph to its launches),
    `codegen_cuda::forward_shim::ForwardShim` (the older forward's answers, `kernel_refusal`'s words) and
    `spatial::shim::SpatialShim`.
  - `tensor_graph::smt` (OPT-5): what a graph computes as terms over arrays — the ops that move values as their index
    maps, every other op a function left open, an in-place write a store into rows or units. `obligation(reference,
    rewritten)` prints the SMT-LIB obligation that a rewrite keeps the meaning for every input and every meaning of the
    open ops — the pairs of terms left once both sides' shared terms and open functions are matched, each a lemma — its
    range facts assumed and cited; `identity(lhs, rhs)` computes products, elementwise sums and products
    and scales exactly, for an algebraic identity at a shape. `fuse::steps(shim)` names every step a compile under a
    shim can hold, in the order it runs them.
  - Numerics contracts (OPT-6): `Shim::numerics` declares the contract of the knob point a chip answers for
    (`tensor_graph::numerics::Contract`): the `Scheme` its products read activations in (the block, how its scale is
    chosen, its int8 words), how it keeps a cache's rows and a recurrent memory (`Keep`), how far a fused op may lie from
    its parts (`Bound`), and its integer accumulator's width. Each scheme and storage format states its `bound`. The CUDA
    lane, the older forward, the spatial chips and the reference declare theirs, and the D5005 engine its build's:
    `EngineShim` carries its `EngineNumerics`, its caches' int8 rows declared as `Keep::Int8Rows` and `Int8RowsRotated`.
  - Integer accumulators proven from ranges (OPT-6): `tensor_graph::facts::accumulator` gives the interval every
    partial result of an integer product, sum or requant lies in (`Accumulates`; `product_sums`), and
    `numerics::holds_accumulator` judges it against a chip's width: the lane, the older forward and the spatial chips
    refuse an integer sum past their accumulator, or one no range bounds below 64 bits, by name. The spatial pass's
    matmul claim reads the same fact.
  - Fused equals apart (OPT-6): the numerics fold (`fused_quant`) is a step a compile holds like any rule
    (`fuse::steps` lists it; held, every quantize stands alone, each fold reported held). The older forward lowers with
    steps held (`qwen_emit::forward_graph_holding`, `compile::compile_to_cuda_forward_holding`), and its runs are
    exact against their kernels apart. The spatial chips declare the tolerance their joins of an approximate program
    are held to (`spatial::equiv::APPROX_REL_TOL`); an exact program's joins are exact.
  - An attention's numerics (OPT-6): `Contract::attention` (`AttentionNumerics`, `Taken`) says how an attention takes its
    queries, keys and values and its weights where a chip narrows them: the lane's f16 tensor-core attention under one
    word, its two-word one under two (each row in two words under its largest over 127, the weights in three;
    `LaneShim::attention`), the D5005 array's (its head's query as an activation, its weights unsigned under a power of
    two a block). Schemes gain `Block::Row`, `Scale::LeastPowerOfTwoOver255` and three words.
  - The shared side of five tier-1 parity fusions: a projection folds a post-norm before its residual add as well as the
    add-and-norm (`gemv_norm_epilogue` over `NormThenAdd`, Gemma's two norms in one fold); a plain norm whose output one
    add reads folds the add into its row op, the norm written on for an op after the add (`sandwich_norm`); a product's
    epilogue takes a constant row or a constant of its shape as its scale, and a bias after the scale
    (`gemm_scale`). `LaneShim::fits` refuses the plain norm-then-add by name until the lane's kernel takes it.
  - A decode layer's query, key and value as one op with each head's norm, rotation and cache write in its epilogue
    (PARITY-QKV-HEAD-EPILOGUE's shared side): a rotation with no per-head norm folds into its projection
    (`qkv_head_epilogue`, Llama's and gpt-oss's), and `merged_projections` takes a projection folded with its heads'
    epilogues as one member (`projects`: a fold whose every product reads the one activation at the one depth),
    checking every buffer a member reads and writes against the ops it moves past.
  - A one-row gated delta rule's whole step between its projections as one op (PARITY-GDN-DECODE-WHOLE's shared side,
    `gdn_decode_whole`): the convolutions of its query, key and values with their SiLU and L2 norms, its two gates, the
    update of its memory and its gated norm; the output's quantize joins it through `fused_quant` as
    `gdn_output_quant`, which no longer stands for the whole step in `realizes`. `gdn_post_conv_prep` groups a chunk's
    convolutions and gates read only by each other and the rule, whatever makes their inputs.
  - `moe_deferred_finalize` also folds a mixture summed with its shared expert's product before the residual add (a
    chunk's token-sorted mixture, whose shared expert stays apart) into the next add and norm
    (PARITY-MOE-DEFERRED-FINALIZE's shared side).
- The CUDA lane links the shared table (MIGRATE-1): `lane::plan` optimizes the step under `LaneShim`, links each op
  the optimizer leaves (`lane_link`), then schedules the launches (four rows a warp, the shared-row attention, the
  split counts, a batch's sequences as one launch) and gives every tensor its buffers. The lane's own rule table, its
  twenty rule functions, its quantize placement and its op-to-launch lowering are deleted; every lane program is
  byte-identical. `lane::graph::StepGraph` is a view of the lifted IR, whose `GraphError`, `Role` and `StepIo` it
  shares; `LaneError::NoKernel` names the link's reason.
- The CUDA lane's folds (`lane::plan::Launch::Fold`): one launch runs a kernel's work and then another's over what it
  wrote, in the blocks that finish what the second reads, each fold computing what its two launches compute apart, bit
  for bit — the second's work is the code its own kernel runs (`lk_rmsnorm_row`, `lk_rope_head`, `lk_gather_q8_at`,
  `lk_gather_sub`, `lk_gather_f32_at`):
  - `embed_norm` (PARITY-EMBED-NORM): the embedding's gather norms each row it gathers in the same block, for every
    table format the gathers read and Gemma's scale, a prefill chunk's rows too (`lk_gather_norm`).
  - `qkv_head_epilogue` (PARITY-QKV-HEAD-EPILOGUE): a step's query, key and value projections in parts turn each head
    — its norm, its rotation, the key into its cache row — in the block that writes the head's last rows, counted at a
    per-head arrival count that block resets (`lk_gemv_parts_rope`); the lane's `gemv_parts` takes such a fold as a
    member, and a query projection four rows a warp turns its heads alone.
  - `gemv_norm_epilogue` (PARITY-GEMV-NORM-EPILOGUE): a Q8_0 projection of a step's one row takes the residual add and
    the next norm — Gemma's post-norm too — into its last-arriving block (`lk_gemv_q8_norm`), and one in any block
    format the library decodes the same way, four rows a warp where its shape takes them, the block's warps playing the
    norm's (`lk_gemv_blk_norm`).
  - `moe_deferred_finalize` (PARITY-MOE-DEFERRED-FINALIZE): a mixture — alone, or summed with its shared expert's
    product first — finishes the residual add and the next norm in its last step: the decode step's down projection in
    its last-arriving block (`lk_moe_down_norm`), a chunk's or a batch's combine a block of the norm's threads a row
    (`lk_moe_combine_norm`). `Launch::finished_mixture` and `Launch::mixture_mut` reach a fold's mixture, which the
    schedule's rows a warp and router splits set as they set a mixture alone; a fold's `parts` flatten nested folds, and
    its scratch is its first part's and its count.
  - `attn_output_quant` (PARITY-ATTN-QUANT-IN-FOLD): the decode step's and a short chunk's attention quantizes its
    output in its own store, each 32-value group in a half-warp (`lk_quant_pair`, `lk_attn_store`), where a head is
    whole groups (`Launch::Attention::{f32, quant}`); a batch's sequences each quantize their row in their store too.
  - `row_write_quant` (PARITY-BATCH-ROW-QUANT): the shared numerics pass quantizes a table every row of which is set,
    once and in whole blocks, from a result its producer can quantize in its epilogue, a row at a time: each producer
    takes its row's quantize and each set the table's, carrying its rows' quantized form (`Launch::SetRow::{f32,
    quant}`, `lk_set_row_q`). The lane's `sequences` lays a family out in the table's quantized form too, so a batch's
    merged attention or delta rule quantizes every row in its store; a batch's gated attentions take their product's
    quantize (`glu_act_mul_quant` over them). A producer writes `f32`, and a set copies values, only where something
    reads the table so.
  - `attn_output_gate` (K-13): a gated attention's output, read by nothing but its product with its gate's activation,
    is multiplied by that activation in the attention's store, before it is quantized (`Launch::Attention::gate`,
    `lk_qout`). A batch's attentions — each sequence's result set into its row of one table that the product gates
    whole — are one op of the class too (`fuse::gates_rows`), linked as each sequence's attention gating its row by its
    row of the gate (`Launch::Attention::goff`) and run as one attention over the sequences; a chunk in tiles gates
    apart. The shared table's `qk_norm_rope_gate_split` finds the gate inside the attention that gates in its store.
  A fold's reads leave out what its second part reads of its first's results (`Launch::reads`, `Launch::touches`), and
  `Launch::parts` gives its kernels' launches to a question about one kernel (the residence order asks it). The
  shared `gemv_norm_epilogue` builds the one-row class alone; a chunk's is `prefill_norm_epilogues`, a rule of its own.
  `lk_rmsnorm_row` runs on a block of any width as the norm's own block of `vt` threads computes it, each warp playing
  up to `VW` of the norm's (`lk_warps_sum`), so a fold's last block norms as the norm's launch does. Decode launches a
  token: Gemma-3-1B 238 → 133, Llama-3.2-1B 148 → 83, Qwen3-8B 328 → 183, Qwen3.5-0.8B 220 → 165, gpt-oss-20b
  196 → 99, DeepSeek-V2-Lite 327 → 274.
- The gated delta rule stepped a row at a time norms each head in the step kernel's last block for that head:
  `lk_delta_step` given the norm's operands updates the memory, gates the norm and quantizes in one kernel
  (`lk_delta_norm_row`, its warps playing the norm's), with a count a head in the arena (K-10).
- The spatial chips link `elementwise_regions` whole (`SpatialShim`): one section whose members, each linked alone, are
  joined from the region's result back, every value it makes handed to the member that reads it and never stored.
- `elementwise_regions` takes a connected region of pointwise ops of one length and element type — a row-broadcast
  bias inside it, a member a later op reads written out beside the result — and `ForwardShim` links it whole: the
  older forward's `k{i}_region` kernel, its products rounded alone (`__fmul_rn`) as the kernels apart round them.
- `tensor_graph::graph::Tensor::carried`: a state the step carries to the next (the front end's `carried_state`).
  `last_layer_rows` keeps every op that writes one out of a chunk's output row, and takes a token-sorted mixture, which
  routes each row on its own.
- The builtins `sealBox`, `openBox`, `sealValueBox` and `openValueBox`: XChaCha20-Poly1305 boxes whose seals draw a
  24-byte nonce from the system's entropy (`BuiltinEffect::Nondeterministic`, so the optimizer never merges two),
  appended at the end of `BuiltinId` (wire ids 265–268). `session.lg`'s `sameTextInConstantTime`; `crypto.lg`'s
  `sha256Of` and `hmacSha256Of` (the digests of bytes already checked). `concurrency::pnp::{PadLedger, LedgerError,
  RECV_WINDOW_COVERS, frame_key_slot}`, `FileLedger::beside`, `PadPool::session_with_ledger`, `Role::name`, and
  `concurrency::channel::PQ_REPLAY_WINDOW`.
- `crypto.lg`: `mldsaSignCtxLogos` and `mldsaVerifyCtxLogos` (ML-DSA under a FIPS 204 context), and the argument
  contract its post-quantum and FIPS-202 functions keep, with the native kernels' reports (`cryptoRequireBytes`,
  `cryptoRequireByteString`, `cryptoRequireOutputLength`, `cryptoAllBytes`, `cryptoIsByteString`,
  `mlkemEncapsKeyValid`, `mldsaRequireSecretKey`, `mldsaReduceRow`). `NativeKernel::failure`, the report a refused
  kernel call stops with on every tier; the Rust backend turns a kernel's `Err` into the same panic.
- Five decode fusions in the lane's rule table (`codegen_cuda::lane::plan::RULES`), each computing what its launches
  compute apart, bit for bit:
  - `gemv_parts` (applied once quantization is placed): projections of one activation's rows within one GEMV's
    (`GEMV_ROWS`), in one format, at one number of rows a warp, launch as one GEMV over their outputs side by side
    (`Launch::GemmParts`, `lk_gemv_parts`): a layer's query, key and value, and a recurrent layer's gate too.
  - `sandwich_norm`: a norm of a sum whose added operand is a norm's whole output read by nothing else (Gemma's post-norms)
    runs inside the sum's norm (`Launch::Norm::pre`, `lk_rmsnorm`'s `PRE`).
  - `conv_parts`: a step's causal convolutions of one row through the SiLU (a recurrent layer's query and key, each head
    normed, and its value) as one launch over their channels (`Launch::ConvParts`, `lk_conv_parts`).
  - `gate_projections`: a recurrent layer's two gates and the two `f32` projections of its activation they read as one
    launch, each head's two dots cut over warps and summed as the GEMV cuts and sums them (`Launch::GateProj`,
    `lk_gate_proj`; the cut `gemv_f32_parts`, set with the projections' own).
  - `rope_parts`: a step's query and key rotations of one row by one table at one position as one launch over both
    tensors' heads (`Launch::RopeParts`, `lk_rope_parts`), the key's straight into its cache row.
  A decode step's launches (`model_lane --plan`): Qwen3.5-0.8B 370 to 220, Gemma-3-1B 368 to 238, Llama-3.2-1B 196 to
  148, Qwen3-8B 436 to 328.
- The CUDA lane decodes a batch: `lane::compile_batch` (and `compile_batch_holding`) compiles a model's batch cut
  (`Cut::Batch`, `author::decode_batch`: `B` sequences a token each, every weight read once for all of them) beside its
  decode step and prefill chunks (`FormKind::{Decode, Prefill, Batch}`, `Lane::batch`, `batch_io`; a batch of one is
  `LaneError::Batch`). The program's `--batch <file>` prefills each prompt into its own sequence's state, then decodes
  every sequence a token a step in the batch step, printing each sequence's tokens and the aggregate `DECODE_TOK_PER_S`.
  The planner's last rule, `sequences`, lays each family of per-sequence arrays (`X_s<b>`) out as one array of them (or
  the rows of the table they are set into) and runs each layer's per-sequence launches alike — delta rules, gates,
  convolutions, attentions, latent-attention joins, cache-row writes, rotations and the arg-max — as one launch whose
  rows are the sequences, each reading its own position and caches (`lk_delta_step`, `lk_conv_parts`, `lk_attention*`,
  `lk_join`, `lk_set_row`, `lk_rope_parts`, `lk_argmax` take a sequence stride). The batch step's census is then the
  same at every batch size: Llama-3.2-1B 164 launches, Qwen3.5-0.8B 280, Gemma-3-1B 264, Qwen3-8B 364, gpt-oss-20b
  220, DeepSeek-V2-Lite 488. Every sequence decodes in the batch exactly as it decodes alone, tokens and logits bit for
  bit, at any batch size: a batch's projections are marked (`batch` on `Launch::{Gemv, GemvF32, GemmParts, GemvGlu,
  Moe, HeadGemv}`, `Launch::is_batch`) and never take the tensor cores' GEMM, the tiled per-head matmul or the tiled
  attention a chunk of as many rows takes — whose sums run in another order, and whose attentions know one sequence's
  caches — but sum each row as the decode step sums its one row. Under the quantized numerics a batch's Q8_0
  projections, merged projections and gated pairs of two rows and more run on the int8 tensor cores in exactly that
  order (`lk_gemv_mma`, `Launch::batch_on_tensor_cores`; a gated pair as its gate and up apart, then `lk_swiglu` or
  `lk_binary` as the pair's own epilogue gates them). `examples/model_lane.rs --batch=<B>` benches the batch
  (`--bench`) and prints its census (`--plan`, `BATCH_LAUNCHES:`). On the RTX 3060, every sequence's tokens a second at
  4, 16, 32 and 64 sequences: Llama-3.2-1B Q8_0 855, 3,007, 4,751, 6,376; Qwen3.5-0.8B 996, 2,683, 3,482, 3,954;
  Gemma-3-1B 922, 3,272, 5,314, 6,825.
- The Q8_0 GEMV's quantized sums take one order every kernel that sums as it sums can take on the tensor cores: each
  32-value block's int8 dot exact in int32, its step into partial `b mod 4` of block `b` (`lk_q8_term`, `lk_q8_acc`,
  each operation spelled out), a partial's blocks in order, the four partials summed `(p0 + p2) + (p1 + p3)`
  (`lk_q8_sum4`). `lk_q8_rows_dot` takes a lane pair a block of each 512 values and chains a partial's steps by
  `shfl_down` over one activation row, and through the warp's scratch (`lk_q8_scratch`) over more, up to eight dots
  at a time; `lk_gemv_dot` names the block's warps (`WARPS`). On the RTX 3060 the decode step's rate is unchanged or
  better (Llama-3.2-1B 233.1 tok/s, Qwen3.5-0.8B 301.4, Gemma-3-1B 239.2, Qwen3-8B 40.51), and a chunk's GEMV of 4 to
  16 rows runs up to 1.4 times as fast.
- `model::gguf::Gguf::decode_read_bytes`: the weight bytes one decode step reads a token — every tensor of the model's
  blocks and around them, the token embedding only where it is the output matrix too, each expert matrix in the
  share of experts a token is routed to, none of the multi-token-prediction draft blocks. `examples/model_lane.rs
  --plan` prints it (`DECODE_READ_BYTES:`) and whether the step routes through experts (`DECODE_ROUTED:`), and the
  decode step's launches: their count and census by kind (`DECODE_LAUNCHES:`) and each in order with what it writes
  (`DECODE_STEP:`).
- The lane's program times each prefill chunk launch by launch under `--profile` too: a `PROFILE_CHUNK: <rows> <kind>
  <output> <µs>` line a launch and a `PROFILE_CHUNK_TOTAL_US` a chunk, beside the decode step's `PROFILE:` lines.
- `model::program`: a model's one program by its forms (`Cut`: decode step, prefill chunk, batch, a layer of each,
  embedding, head, and the split forms over tensor-parallel chips; `Cut::write`, `Cut::write_split`) and the identity of
  each (`Identity`: the cut, the chips, the sha256 of the authored text; `Identity::line`), with `written`,
  `written_in` and `written_split`. The CUDA lane's forms carry their identity (`lane::Form::identity`) and its `.cu`
  prints it before each form's source.
- `spatial::decode_engine::program::lower(spec, numerics, ctx)` reads the engine's program off the model's decode step
  (author, shared front end, step graph, `lower_graph`), the lift remembered per spec and context, and returns it with
  its identity (`Lowered`, `ProgramError`); the hand lowering is `program::hand_lowering`, an oracle. `Machine` carries
  the identity, `ofs::write_program` writes `program.lg` and `program.txt` beside the program, and
  `capability::Refusal::Program` names a decode step the lowering refuses.
- `codegen_cuda::qwen38::Qwen38Cfg::from_spec`: the Qwen3.8 configuration a checkpoint's spec describes, refused
  unless its decode step's identity is the checkpoint's. The SN50 streamed deployment reads every program through
  `model::program` (`StreamedModel::{embed_identity, head_identity}`, `LayerKind::identity`, `LayerBinding::identity`),
  and `RduBundle` gains `program`, the decode step's text, named by the manifest's `program` line.
- `bringup`: the chip interface a model's one program is brought up through (`Backend`, `Brought`, `Refused`; `Vm`,
  `Jit`, `C`, `Rust`, `RiscvQemu`, `Wasm`, `Cuda`, `D5005`, `Sn50`, `Fabric`; `backends`), and `bring_up` /
  `bring_up_spec`, which write the program and run the backends asked for. A D5005 refusal is the engine's gaps, each
  once with its layers (`capability::summarize`). `bring_up` reads a GGUF file or a Hugging Face directory
  (`model::open_checkpoint`, `ModelSpec::from_source`); a directory writes the program its converted GGUF does.
  `bringup::run_sequences` and `Backend::run`: every chip that brought a model up runs every `Sequence` it is given (a
  prompt and the tokens it is judged against) from an empty state and reports `Generated` (a list of tokens a
  sequence, its seconds, what ran it, the program it ran); `bringup::run` is the one-sequence case, reporting `Ran`.
  Each is judged by `agreeing` against the model's f64 reference (`Reference::of`, over either kind of checkpoint) or
  a golden (`Reference::golden`: its first prompt; `Reference::golden_sequences`: every prompt with its own
  continuation). A CPU tier builds one generation program and runs it once: every weight declared and read once
  through `readReals`, each sequence's state (the caches, memories and rings its binding names state) declared
  afresh, and the decode step's own statements — every line but the bindings of its token and position, whose names
  the run reads off the step — in a loop over the positions, the prompt fed a token a step and each token after it the
  step's own `next`. The VM and the JIT run it in process; C, Rust, RISC-V and WebAssembly build it once.
  `Cuda::run` builds the lane with `nvcc` for this machine's GPU once and runs each sequence, timed by its own clocks;
  `D5005::run` runs the engine's machine model when no card is here, a machine a sequence; `Sn50::run` refuses
  (SambaNova's SDK is gated); the fabric refuses by name, as it does not lower a model the author writes yet.
- `examples/model_lane.rs --repeats=N`: N timed runs after a warm-up, each printed, then their mean and sample
  deviation, the compiled lane's provenance line and each program identity it ran.
- `spatial::decode_engine::ofs`: `AfuConfig::flat`, a tree compiled into the FIM itself, its sources.txt naming the
  FIM's static exercisers' list (`FIM_STATIC_EXERCISERS`, under `OFS_ROOTDIR`); `d5005_afu --flat`.
- `spatial::decode_engine::rtl::streaming`: `logos_act_blocks_p`, the activation quantizer over blocks back to back (a
  ring of the blocks' beats, a queue of their scales, each block's codes out a beat a clock in order, zero past its
  length, as `logos_act_stream_p`'s now are whatever block came before), `ACT_BLOCKS_READ_LATENCY` and
  `act_blocks_out`, its clocks; the streaming matvec reads every block of every
  token-row in one walk into it and writes each block's codes into the array's activations as it gives them.
- `spatial::decode_engine::rtl::gdn`: `logos_gdn_side` reads its return queue's head a clock ahead into registers (a
  line written the clock it is read passed straight through), so no path from the head starts at the queue's arrays;
  every valid of the GDN units in registers the reset clears, none riding a delay line beside its data; the side asks
  the flush's out-stream against its write queue's room over the asks still on their way.
- `spatial::decode_engine::machine::VectorJob::kept` and `vector::Op::writes`: a load of a tensor at stride 0 into a
  register nothing else of the job writes, flagged in its load word (`[31:24]`; `decode` refuses a flag that says
  otherwise) and loaded on the job's first pass alone by the machine, whose registers now hold from pass to pass as
  the vector unit's do, and by the vector unit's side.
- `spatial::decode_engine::image`: `ImageLayout::slot` and `Placement::{pad, slot_bytes, image_row_bytes}`, each block
  of a striped tensor laid in a slot of a whole number of `slot` bytes, its pad zeros; `rtl::column`:
  `ColumnSet::{slot, with_slots, slot_bytes}`, a slotted set's feeds gathering a beat a slot at a time and its
  signature naming the slot (`@16`); `StreamEntry::of` refuses an image laid in slots of another size than its set's,
  and `Streaming::new` a set and an image that lay blocks otherwise. The `d5005_afu` example and
  `scripts/d5005/build-afu.sh` take `--slots <n>`, and `--hard-float <measurement>`: the fast side's f32 units on the
  blocks the oracle's measurement on the card names (`fp_hard::Measurement::read`), or `--hard-adds <measurement>`: its
  adds alone (`HardFloat::product` is optional, `hard_float_verilog` keeping the soft multiplies), the card's block
  flushing a tiny product before it rounds.
- `spatial::decode_engine::rtl::column`: `ColumnSet::{octets, with_octets, octet, block_len, block_bytes, act_slots}`,
  `DecodedFields::s7`; a set with octets builds `logos_dec_q8_0x8`, its core's octet slots and its array's emitter, and
  no lone column; `logos_column_act_word` takes `SLOTS` scales at `slot`.
- `spatial::decode_engine::image::ImageLayout::octets`: a streamed Q8_0 tensor's rows laid eight blocks to one of the
  array's, refused with `ImageError::RaggedRow` where a row is not whole octets; `StreamEntry::of` refuses an image laid
  in blocks of another size than its set's.
- **RTL as circuits, timed and pipelined before Quartus runs.**
  - `codegen_sva::netlist`: Verilog to an AIG and its word-level JSON through yosys, every multiplier lowered by one
    fixed map (`CANONICAL_MUL`) so a product is the same circuit in every design, cells computing one thing from the
    same signals merged once every undefined bit is its own; an undefined bit free (`synthesize`) or 0
    (`synthesize_reading` with `Undefined::Zero`), read again once cells are lowered (a part-select at a moving
    offset is undefined past its operand only then); several clocks or a level latch refused by name;
    `synthesize_cut` cuts the named units out (`expose -evert`), `CutNetlist::units` hands them to
    `logicaffeine_proof::units`, each unit's function its module and the parameters its instance gives, read from the
    RTLIL netlist with their widths and signs (`UnitInstance::function`, `u #(K=-32'sd1)`), so one module at two
    values is two functions and a parameter left at its default is the module's own; a unit's operands and results
    are every bit the cut exposes for its instance, past those it connects when its module declares the port wider.
    Every synthesis hands yosys only the modules its top (and its cut units) reach and reads them deferred, each
    elaborated only as the hierarchy instantiates it, a cut's units as their ports alone (`read_verilog -lib`): one
    channel of the streaming matvec synthesizes in 6 s, from 100 s, and a streaming column with its datapath cut in
    37 s, from 212. `clock_sources`:
    every net that clocks a register or memory port, driven by a port, a register or logic.
  - `codegen_sva::timing_graph`: a module's retiming graph (adders and comparisons as carry chains, products as DSP
    blocks, clocked reads as block RAMs, a RAM read held under an enable as the RAM's own register, an array written
    through more than two ports as registers, everything else mapped to six-input LUTs by `codegen_sva::lutmap`);
    `Delays` and each edge's `Cost`, `reweigh`, `depth` with the deepest path's edges, `endpoints` (the deepest path
    into each register and output: each stage of a pipeline), `loop_bound` by `cycle_ratio`, and `fit`, the delays
    that best match measured depths (their ratios choose the critical paths, the geometric mean of measure over
    depth their scale). A module named a box, or one the source declares `(* blackbox *)`, is timed apart: emptied
    before the hierarchy is elaborated, its instances' inputs end paths (`End::Box`) and their outputs start them, so
    a shell is timed around its engine in a minute. `modules_numbered` names every module of a generated family
    (`--box-memories` boxes every RAM the engine's generator writes as the block RAM it is, its read a memory's delay),
    and a register's controls are read by the flop's polarity, so one held at its idle level is a plain register.
    An adder whose operands are all products or such sums sits inside the DSP blocks, as Quartus packs a block's
    adder and its chain-in: no carry chain and no delay past the product. `Area` counts each instance's LUTs, carry
    bits, DSP halves, flip-flops and memory bits (`area_under` a subtree), and `Calibration` records the model's depth
    and area beside Quartus's, build by build and instance by instance, with the builds it cannot map refused by why,
    and the ALUT fit (`fit_area`: ALUTs as a scale of LUTs plus weighted carry bits). A memory no port reads is
    dropped before memories are collected (yosys 0.33 collects one into a cell its own check refuses).
  - `codegen_sva::autopipe`: the fewest-register pipeline of a combinational module at a period and latency, on named
    nets or on any net, the minimum-area retiming of Leiserson & Saxe solved through its min-cost-flow dual, period
    constraints added only where a schedule breaks them and every node held between its earliest and latest stage;
    `unpipelined` takes a pipeline's registers out so it is cut afresh at its own latency; `check`, and `emit` with
    `Plan::with_outputs_registered`; logic around a box is not cut (`PipeError::Boxed`); a register whose data is a
    constant is cut as that constant, one with live controls is held, and `emit` refuses to write a net it never
    drives.
  - `codegen_sva::quartus`: Quartus reports as a build ledger row — flow, resources, slack per clock, the retimer's
    limits, the router's peaks, the delay the router added for hold per clock transfer, every clock net and whether it
    rides a global network (a fit still running read from its plan and place reports) — judged on what the job ran;
    `Row::synthesis_sources`, the compiles the timing model is calibrated against; each fit's Logic Lock regions
    (`RegionUsage`: what was placed in each over what it holds, and its rectangles); `Row::resumed`, the compile a
    job went on from, whose modules it inherits instead of being judged on them (`quartus_record --resumed`).
  - `codegen_sva::preflight`: a persona build held to the PR region and its clock before Quartus runs — its ALUTs
    by the calibration's area fit, its ALMs at the ratio Quartus's own syntheses packed theirs, its DSP blocks and
    registers, its deepest path and loop bound in nanoseconds, and the fitter's time from the personas' fits; each
    thing over the region or past the period refused by name, past the fullest persona that fit flagged tight.
  - `codegen_sva::floorplan`: a floorplan read off a fitted build. Each instance named gets the least square that holds
    its need (the fitter's own ALMs and M20Ks) at a fill of the parent region's density, every site inside the parent
    region (a persona's rectangles, or the whole device for a flat build), nearest the site its clock comes from (the
    fast signal sharing the clock's target's deepest hierarchy: an EMIF user clock's IO tile control). An instance's
    clock is the one its setup paths run on, both ends inside it. The squares become `PLACE_REGION` settings with
    `CORE_ONLY_PLACE_REGION` on and `RESERVE_PLACE_REGION` off. The `d5005_floorplan` example writes one for every
    memory channel of a build: hybrid3's channels, each 39 M20Ks and run on the opposite bank's 300 MHz clock, in 28×28
    squares at their banks' tiles.
  - `codegen_sva::floorplan` places a build's units in regions of measured capacity. The geometry probe (`tiles`,
    `probe`; example `d5005_geometry_probe`) pins one register to each tile of the device by its own Logic Lock region,
    so one fit records every tile's ALMs, registers, M20Ks and DSP blocks (`Geometry::of`); `Geometry::capacity` sums
    whole tiles only. `fabricless` reads off a probe's fit the tiles with no flip-flop (hard IP, the core's edge: ten of
    the D5005's 342), which `d5005_geometry_probe --without <fit log>` leaves out, so no floorplan region takes one. A
    unit holds in a region at a `Fill`: a share of the logic, the rest left for the router, and a share of the hard M20K
    and DSP blocks. `place` puts each unit, in the order given, in the least rectangle of whole
    tiles (`candidates`, within an aspect, clear of what is blocked) where its wires' widths times the distances
    between centres are least; `grow` gives each a region of whole tiles instead, seeded where its wires are shortest in
    a free area that holds it and grown round the centre of its sites, so it bends round what is taken (`Region::rects`,
    `regions_tcl`: one `PLACE_REGION` of several rectangles). `unit_wires` reads a floorplan's wires off a module's graph
    (`netlist::module_graph`), each anchored unit wired to its anchor's site by the bits it shares with the module's
    ports, each region named for its unit's path (`region_name`). A unit is one instance or several sharing one region
    (`Unit::instances`), its wires theirs to everything outside it; a unit the module lacks, two units of one name, a
    unit of no instance and an instance in two units are refused. `unit_floorplan` and `unit_regions` place on them.
    `anchor` reads where an instance's clock comes from off a fitted build; example `d5005_anchors` records the memory
    channels' banks in `benchmarks/silicon/d5005/emif_anchors.json`. `synthesized_needs` reads what a unit's instances
    need together off its synthesis: their ALUTs and registers summed and packed once at the ledger's packing (the
    fitter packs one member's registers beside another's LUTs), their DSP blocks, the member the report lacks named,
    and the M20K blocks the fitter gives each of their memories (`synthesized_rams`, `Ram::m20k`): a stack of
    blocks a power of two deep, a byte-enabled block holding a byte a 10-bit lane, an `AUTO` memory in MLABs when it is
    small and needs no old data across its ports. On hybrid7b's synthesis that is the fitter's own 1,024 M20Ks for the
    store and 45 for the attention side. Example `d5005_unit_floorplan` writes a build's unit floorplan from the probe's
    ledger row, the anchors, the synthesis report and the tree's Verilog, a `--group <name>=<path>,<path>...` a unit of
    several instances packed together; `--wires` prints the wires the units are placed on, heaviest first, and each
    instance no unit holds by the bits it carries to the units (`floorplan::loose`): a path through one is no wire of
    the floorplan's until its module is dissolved by `--through`.
  - `codegen_sva::preflight::synthesized_holds` holds a synthesized flat image to the device (`DEVICE`, the 1SX280's
    933,120 ALMs, 5,760 DSP blocks and 11,721 M20Ks) by what its synthesis report says it needs: the ALMs and DSP
    blocks every partition's estimate sums to, and the M20K blocks the fitter gives every partition's memories, not
    their bits over a block's; `over` names each resource past the device's. Example `d5005_budget` prints it and exits
    5 on what the device cannot hold: hybrid7b's synthesis needs 5,965 M20Ks by it, against its fit's 5,848.
  - `codegen_sva::netlist::module_graph` reads a module's instances as a floorplan's wires. Two instances' bits are the
    nets one drives and the other reads and what each drives into the other through the module's own logic, the fewer
    of the bits leaving the one and arriving at the other, so a bus through a register stage counts its width and a
    select steering it one bit; a net two instances only read joins neither to the other, so a broadcast is a star
    from its driver (sixteen cores reading one activation bus pull toward it, not each toward the other fifteen), and a
    glue cell's clock pins carry none. The modules named are dissolved into the module, their instances named by
    path, and each instance's bits to the module's own ports are counted the same way.
  - `codegen_sva::fp_hypotheses`: binary32 multiply and add under a family of semantics (denormal inputs and results,
    tininess, the flushed sign, the NaN returned: four canonical NaNs, or the first NaN operand's payload with an ARM
    or an x86 core's default NaN), every member from one generator, and `SPECIALS`; `Semantics::IEEE` is a member.
    Each member's result is one chain of choices, no bit of it undefined; a member that keeps denormal operands
    multiplies the engine's product of the significands with their leading ones, less what a denormal's missing one
    contributes, and normalizes as the engine does unless an operand is denormal, so on denormal-free operands its
    datapath is the engine's.
  - `codegen_sva::fp_hard`: the engine's f32 multiply and add on the card's floating-point DSP block, as the oracle
    measured it. `hard_verilog` (and `hard_verilog_ce`, stepping at an enable) writes a wrapper with the pipelined
    unit's ports and exactly its latency: the block fully registered and padded, or with its output register alone,
    or refused as too deep (`plan`); around it what makes a near-miss block the engine's arithmetic (an add's denormal
    operand flushed before the block, a multiply's decided beside it so the block multiplies what the engine
    multiplies; a denormal result flushed; every NaN `0x7fc00000`); a block whose flushing loses what no logic after it
    can restore refused by name (`HardError`). `Measurement::read` turns the card's measurement into each mode's
    `Block` and the multiply-add's `Rounding` (once, twice, or neither); `read_product` and `read_sum` read one block
    alone, so a multiply no class names leaves the add's block readable; `read_tally` (each class's differing steps
    over every run and the steps no class explains, `Tally`), `read_depths`, `read_runs` and `read_multiply_add` read
    the rest of a mode or of the measurement alone.
  - `codegen_sva::fp_hypotheses`: `Nan::XorSigned`, the default quiet NaN signed by the operands' signs' exclusive
    or, as the card's multiply returns every NaN (`.nan-xor-7fc00000`); the family is 98 members, the discriminator
    ledger 98 product classes and 42 sum classes. `codegen_sva::fp_oracle`'s record gives the product classes two
    words (`MAX_PRODUCT_CLASSES` 128) and the sum classes three halves (`MAX_SUM_CLASSES` 96), the least-registered
    multiply's result beside the flags; the host program reads each field by its word and bit.
  - `codegen_sva::fp_hypotheses::Semantics::{mul, add}`: a member's binary32 multiply and add in software, bit for bit
    what its unit computes (one seam: the engine's arithmetic takes a member from it). `Semantics::ENGINE` is the
    engine's `numerics::fmul`/`fadd`, `Semantics::IEEE` this machine's `f32`; the card's multiply is the engine's but
    for its NaN's sign and the products it flushes that the engine rounds up into the least normal (`0x3f7fffff ×
    0x00800000`: `0x00800000` in the engine, `0` on the card).
  - `codegen_sva::fp_hard::plan_for` plans a block's wrapper to compute any target semantics: the operands and results
    the block keeps denormal where the target flushes them, flushed, the target's `0x7fc00000` written for another NaN,
    and refused by name, the target named, where the block reads a zero or flushes what the target keeps, decides
    tininess elsewhere, flushes to another sign or returns NaNs the wrapper cannot write. `plan` is `plan_for` of
    `Semantics::ENGINE`, word for word; a member planned for itself needs nothing around it. `HardError::Semantics`
    carries its reason as a `String`. `hard_verilog_for` and `hard_verilog_ce_for` write the wrapper computing a
    target (a block's denormal operand decided beside it with the target's NaN); `hard_verilog` and `hard_verilog_ce`
    are them of `Semantics::ENGINE`, byte for byte.
  - `codegen_sva::fp_receipt`: what the card measured of its floating-point DSP block as a receipt. `receipt` reads one
    from a build ledger row that passed (the engine it compiled, by SHA-256), the discriminator ledger, the image's
    SHA-256 and the card run's own files (`gbs.sha256`, `pr.log`, `userclk.log`, `started`, `finished`,
    `measurement.json`): refused when the run configured another image, partial reconfiguration reported an error, no
    user clock was set, the run's times are out of shape or order, or the measurement did not finish. Each mode is its
    depths, its one agreeing class or how many agreed, the steps no class explains, the `NEAREST` classes by differing
    steps and the engine's unit at its latency on the block (`Unit::Hard`, `fp_hard::plan`'s build, or
    `Unit::Refused` and why); the multiply-add its depth and rounding. `record` refuses besides an engine `engine_held`
    does not hold and a measurement the ledger holds, and appends the receipt: the engine given must be the one the
    build compiled (its SHA-256 the row's), and every module of it the oracle writes itself (`Oracle::own_verilog`: its
    units, vector ROM, delay line and core) the text the discriminator ledger's oracle writes then, byte for byte, while
    the library it embeds (the engine's crossing and top, the store's memory) is held by the build's SHA-256 alone, so the
    library moves on without voiding a measured build. Its inputs are kept beside the ledger (`KEPT` in `kept_dir`, the
    compiled `logos_engine.v` among them, each as `kept` keeps it: the image's `sha256sum` line naming it by its file
    name alone, so no kept input names a machine's directories); `receipt_of`
    finds the receipt recording a measurement by its SHA-256, so a hard-float build reads its blocks against the
    discriminator ledger kept with it (the `d5005_afu` example's `--hard-float`/`--hard-adds` read their measurement so,
    refusing one no receipt records).
  - `codegen_sva::fp_oracle`: the persona that measures which semantics the card's floating-point DSP block computes.
    An engine build whose engine is the oracle behind the engine's own crossing (`Oracle::engine_verilog`; the shell,
    its commands and its multicycle constraints unchanged): each compute step an operand triple from the discriminator
    vectors, a xorshift stream or the same stream with exponents where the semantics part, into the hard multiply,
    add and multiply-add (`fourteennm_fp_mac` in `sp_mult`, `sp_add` and `sp_mult_add`, each operand its mode reads
    crossing as many registers to the adder — the multiply's and the multiply-add's an input register and two pipeline
    registers, the add's its input register alone, Quartus allowing an add no multiplier pipeline — each connected only
    at the operands its mode reads and at no internal-use port, as Quartus requires of the block and of `fp_hard`'s
    wrappers alike, each block's results delayed the host's steps so blocks of any depths meet the soft side together:
    `Run::aligned`) and, through a delay line at the host's tap, into one soft unit for every class of the
    discriminator ledger (`Oracle::from_ledger`); a counter a class of the results that differ, the steps no class
    explains counted and stored, or every step in a recording run (`Store::read`); beside them a multiply and an add
    with only their output registered, recorded as they leave them. `hard_model_verilog` stands in for the encrypted
    block in simulation (`Depths` deep); `persona_sources` writes the tree, its simulation twin and the measurement the
    card runs (`HOST_PROGRAM`: the multiply's and the add's latencies as the taps at which their classes come nearest
    every vector, so a block no class names still has its depth, the multiply-add's and the least-registered blocks' as the shifts at which their results are the
    aligned ones', then the vectors recorded and both streams counted, one JSON object).
  - Examples: `timing_report` (depth, endpoints with where each spends its delay, instances, loop bound, `--box`,
    `--path` printing an endpoint's deepest path node by node),
    `autopipe_report` (`--cuts`, `--outputs registered`, `--emit`, `--prove` with its progress and its queries by
    cut, `--aiger` writing both sides; a pipeline cut afresh at its own latency; the proof by `fraig`, `--sweep` for
    the cut-by-cut engine), `timing_calibrate` (`--fit`,
    `--netlists`), `quartus_record` (`--again`, `--judge`), `fp_discriminators`, `d5005_step_graph`; and in
    `d5005_preflight` (a design's verdict from its Verilog, exit 1 on a refusal), and in `logicaffeine_proof`,
    `aig_equiv` (two AIGER circuits decided latency-aligned, by `sweep` or `--fraig` with
    `--recycle` and `--collect`, or their miter written for ABC); `rtl_equiv` (two modules of Verilog files or of the
    decode engine's libraries as its generator writes them, each read at its own clock, decided over the units
    `--unit` pairs, each unit's latency on its side, every round's identifications and derivation printed and checked);
    `d5005_fp_oracle` (the hard FP oracle persona's tree for `scripts/d5005/build-afu.sh`, `--model` its simulation's
    stand-in block), `d5005_fp_hard` (a measured block's wrapper, with its stand-in when asked), `d5005_fp_receipt` (a
    card run recorded into the receipts ledger, its inputs kept beside it).
- **`spatial::decode_engine::capability::{needs, covers, runs_on}`**: what a checkpoint's step needs of an engine
  build (its engine parameters and the formats a hybrid build streams), whether a build covers it (the dual of
  `EngineParams::union`: a build covers a step exactly when their union is the build), every gap named, and whether a
  checkpoint's header runs on a build at all. `covers` names every field of the step's engine, so a field
  `EngineParams` gains does not compile until it is judged.
- `model::reference::KvStorage`: an attention cache kept exact (the default) or as llama.cpp's default cache keeps
  it (`with_kv_storage(KvStorage::F16)`): each key and value row rounded to `f32` and then binary16 as it enters, every
  read taking the rounded row; `model::quant::f32_to_f16`, the front door's binary16 rounding (to nearest, ties to
  even), the engine's at every boundary.
- `codegen_sva::preflight::device_library`: the device primitives a design instantiates and does not define, each as
  the box it is (`codegen_sva::fp_oracle::fp_mac_blackbox`: the floating-point DSP block's interface, one DSP block
  large), so a design on the card's hard floating-point blocks preflights (the `d5005_preflight` example appends it);
  `codegen_sva::timing_graph::BOX_AREA`, a box's declared area (`logos_box_area`), added for every instance.
  `preflight::packing`: the ALMs Quartus packs an ALUT and a register into, each over the syntheses that term sets,
  a design's ALMs the larger of the two (register-bound designs pack ~0.7 ALMs a register); the capacity, the fullest
  persona and the fitter's time read from persona compiles alone (`iofs_pr_afu`, the PR slot's region), a flat
  image's floorplan regions never one.
- `spatial::decode_engine::model::EngineNumerics::{real_points_for, fastest_for}`: the knob points a family is held to.
  A model whose every attention layer norms its keys keeps `real_points()` and `fastest()` exactly; one with a layer
  that does not (Llama) holds its two-word int8 cache rotated and its fastest point's attention on the vector units,
  the points Llama-3.2-1B meets its bars at against llama.cpp's runs of its real checkpoint. The `d5005_afu` example
  writes each program and machine at its family's fastest point.
- `spatial::decode_engine::{machine, model}`: a rotation scaled by the checkpoint's frequency factors
  (`RopeScaling::Factors`, Llama 3's `rope_freqs.weight`) runs: the machine's rotary tables and the model's divide
  each pair's angle by its factor (`model::rotation`, `model::rotary_factors`), a rotation without them takes the same
  path as before, and a table that is not one factor a pair is `EngineError::Factors`. `capability` admits it, refusing
  a factored checkpoint without the table (`Refusal::MissingTensor("rope_freqs.weight")`).
- **`spatial::decode_engine::lower_graph`**: the engine's program read off a model's step graph block by block, each
  op checked by kind, dataflow and checkpoint binding, the projections a block splits one matrix into streamed as
  one matvec; what the engine has no unit for refused by name. `d5005_lower_graph_lock`: the program it reads is the
  hand lowering's, command by command and word by word, at every knob point, on the tiny engine models, the 27B's
  header ledgers and the real checkpoints; every other architecture refused by its block's name.
