  - Value types: money quantized to its ISO-4217 minor unit, quantities with an exact magnitude and dimension vector, UUIDs (v3/v5), Word8–Word64 ring operations, each with `Seq of` and `Map of Text to` families; temporal extractors, `makeDate`/`makeMoment`, `parse_timestamp`/`format_timestamp`, and `today`/`now` reading `LOGOS_FIXED_DAYS`/`LOGOS_FIXED_NANOS` when set (`type_tier_coverage_lock`, `c_temporal_lock`).
  - Aggregates: tuples, named records, `Seq of` a record, tuple or `Seq`, `Map of Text to Seq of …`, and `Repeat for (a, b) in`, emitted in four phases (forward declarations, containers, by-value bodies in dependency order, every helper prototyped before any is defined); maps and sets keep insertion order through rehash and show as `{k: v}` and `{a, b}`; `length of` a map, `union` and `intersection` (`c_seq_of_tuple_lock`, `c_web_json_forward_decl_lock`, `c_collections_cross_tier`).
  - A closure literal is a `static` C function passed by pointer to `mapSeq`/`filterSeq`/`reduceSeq`/`anySeq`/`allSeq`; a scalar higher-order function whose call sites may overflow takes `LInt` per call site, the same promotion AOT-Rust uses (`higher_order_bignum_lock`, `exact_hof_call_site_lock`).
  - `ConvergentCount`/`Tally` fields are single-replica counters, `Append` to a `SharedSequence` and `Resolve` on a register lower, and `SharedSet`/`SharedMap` are refused by type name (`c_crdt_lock`); `Listen`/`Connect`/`Send`/`Stream`/`Await` run over the offline loopback; `Check that …` is an inline guard failing with the policy's message; `Swap`, `During`, `Reversibly:` and bare `{n:5}` widths lower.
  - `Fail with …` and `Require`/`Assert`/`Assume … , otherwise <reason>` stop with exactly the reason on stderr and exit 1 (one task under the scheduler) through `logos_fatal`, the one failure path (`failure_all_tiers`).
  - Text builtins `text_bytes`, `text_from_bytes`, `chr` and `item k of` a Text lower, as do `length(x)` and `copy(x)`, `not` on a Text (emptiness), and the Bool results of `&`, `|` and `xor`.
  - Limits: an expression C does not lower emits an `#error` naming the construct, while a statement kind with no C lowering still falls through to an `/* unsupported stmt */` comment. Where the interpreter promotes to a big integer and the overflow is not provable from literals or a growing accumulator, C's `int64_t` arithmetic wraps modulo 2^64; that divergence is pinned in `crates/logicaffeine_tests/tests/gauntlet/c_known_divergences.txt`, and C's remaining compile gaps (a closure value bound to a name and called) in `crates/logicaffeine_tests/tests/gauntlet/c_known_compile_gaps.txt` (`ultimate_gauntlet_lock`).
  - `codegen_c::{codegen_monomorphic_program_c, TaskTier, SCHED_CORE, USER_CODE_SENTINEL}`, and `codegen_c::cshake::{CUnit, UnitKind, c_identifiers, parse_c_units, reachable_units}`, which tree-shakes a C translation unit to what its roots reach.
- **Concurrent programs on the C family run on one scheduler core.**
  - `crates/logicaffeine_compile/src/codegen_c/sched_core.c` ports `logicaffeine_runtime`'s `process_step` to C one operation at a time: every concurrency operation is a scheduling point, the chooser is the runtime's SplitMix64 seeded from `LOGOS_SEED` (`crates/logicaffeine_compile/src/codegen_c/host_seed.c` reads it as `logicaffeine_system` does), a default pipe holds 32, quiescence with a task still waiting is a deadlock, and a failing task fails the run through `logos_fatal`'s hook while the others finish.
  - Host C and RISC-V (`codegen_riscv::RISCV_RUNTIME_C`) run it as coroutines under `ucontext_tier.c`. `compile_to_c_stepped` and `compile_to_c_stepped_with_knobs` compile each task to step functions (`logos_frame_<f>`, `logos_step_<f>`) under the stepped scheduler (`sched_core.c`, `opt.c`, `stackless_tier.c`, `stepped_tier.c`), the tier CUDA and the bytecode walker use; a failing statement fails its task (`LOGOS_STEP_FAIL`), and a runtime helper's failure longjmps to the step's entry on the host.
  - `codegen_c::concurrency::{SchedulerKnobs, CTarget, ConcurrencyLowering, ConcurrencyTradeoff}` re-export `SchedulerConfig`, `SchedulePolicy` and `ClockMode`; `SchedulerKnobs::emit_defines` prints the policy, clock, pipe capacity and seed; with `LOGOS_SCHED_WORKERS` above 1 the stepped host runs a pthread pool whose output does not depend on the worker count. `ui_bridge::{run_vm_concurrent_with_config, run_vm_concurrent_traced_with_config}` run the VM at the same `SchedulerConfig` as the oracle for these knobs.
  - A receive, a select arm, `Try to receive` and `Await … into` produce a value-or-nothing (`logos_opt`) of any scalar element in its packing (a Real by its bits, a Text by its pointer); an `Int` read of one is `logos_opt_int`, which fails the task on `nothing`, and `logos_opt_eq`/`logos_opt_eq_real`/`logos_opt_eq_text` compare by element. `opt.c` holds the value-or-nothing and Real-bits helpers every C-family tier shares; RISC-V guards the same reads with a flag slot per register that may hold `nothing`.
  - A pipe handle knows its element type: `analysis::pipes::{pipe_params, declare_pipe_params}` find, to a fixpoint over hand-offs, which parameters carry a pipe, `compile_to_c` declares each as `Pipe of T`, and a send packs by `T`. A task entry takes one argument array (`logos_spawn(fn, nargs, args)`) packed by the callee's parameter types, a Real by its bits; the stepped tier's frames take the same argument cells.
  - Tests: `c_family_scheduler_conformance_lock`, `c_scheduler_port_lock`, `stepped_tier_lock`, `c_task_abort_lock`, `c_concurrency_knob_lock`, `c_bytecode_concurrency_lock`.
- **A bytecode-to-C walker.** `compile_to_c_via_bytecode`, `compile_to_c_via_bytecode_with_knobs` and `codegen_c::from_program::{compile_program_to_c, compile_program_to_c_with_policy}` lower the optimized `CompiledProgram` op by op over one `int64_t` register file whose per-register kind selects each operation: big integers, rationals, sequences, sets, maps, tuples, records, enums, CRDT counters, the offline network, policy checks, and a real-socket TCP client whose `Err` text is the VM's. A concurrent program lowers through the stepped tier under its `SchedulerKnobs`; an unmodeled kind is a typed refusal. `largo emit c` keeps the AST emitter. `c_bytecode_lock`, `c_bytecode_concurrency_lock`, `tcp_walker_lock`, `c_bytecode_perf_lock`.
- **RISC-V ahead-of-time tier.** `codegen_riscv::codegen_program_riscv` and `compile::compile_to_riscv` lower the register bytecode (after `vm::peephole`) to RV64GC assembly for the SysV lp64d ABI, one stack slot per VM register, linked with the C shim `codegen_riscv::RISCV_RUNTIME_C`. 115 of the 117 ISA ops lower; `Tcp` and `Serve` need host sockets and are refused with `RiscVLowerError::Unsupported`. A collection changes in place while its register is its only holder: every register a mutation targets carries an owned flag in its frame, which an op that makes a fresh collection sets and any op that could leave the handle elsewhere (another register, a global, a field, an element, an iterator, a closure, a channel) clears, and a push, pop, indexed write, map insert, set add or remove or field write copies first only while it is clear (`codegen_riscv::mutates_own_collection`, the runtime's `_own` helpers). `riscv_aot_lock::a_change_reaches_only_the_holder_it_was_made_through` holds the value semantics and `riscv_aot_lock::every_owned_mutation_runs_in_linear_memory` runs a long loop of each such mutation within a 512 MB address space. `riscv_aot_lock` classifies every op with an empty gap ledger and runs the corpus, the error corpus, the concurrent and network oracles and the 70 benchmark programs under `qemu-riscv64` against the VM, each program at up to four sizes no larger than its smallest declared workload and every run bounded to 512 MB and 60 s; the benchmark half runs from `scripts/run-qemu-corpus-tests.sh`. The tier runs what the VM runs, as the VM runs it: only the functions a program can enter are lowered (`vm::reach`); `Option`, `Result` and declared enums match by constructor name; closures, tuples, sets and maps show, compare, iterate and deep-copy as on the VM; `Merge`/`Converge` change the target's own record field by field as `crdt_merge_field` does; an index counts from the back when negative; every failure stops the program with the VM's own sentence, taken at compile time from the function the VM fails with (`semantics::policy::check_policy`, `semantics::failcode`, the `arith` and builtin refusals); and a function that finishes without a value answers `nothing`. A list or counter in a record's field is the record's own value: a field op changes it in place while the register owns the record and the register's field flag says nothing else holds the value, and copies first otherwise, the flag kept by an alias and liveness analysis of the registers that read the value out (`emit::Mutation::Field`). `riscv_instruction_budget_lock` holds the instructions the emitted code of 64 programs executes to shrink-only budgets, and the runtime's call-frame information lives in `.debug_frame`, so startup registers no frames.
- **CUDA ahead-of-time tier.** `codegen_cuda::codegen_program_cuda` and `compile::compile_to_cuda` emit one self-contained `.cu` that runs a whole program on one GPU thread, with a host launcher, a `Makefile` and the C tier's oracle.
  - The runtime slice a program references is tree-shaken out of the C tier (`codegen_c::cshake`) and lifted `__device__`, with device replacements for what a GPU thread lacks: a Dragon4 shortest-round-trip `f64` formatter (`codegen_cuda::LOGOS_DEVICE_FLOAT_FMT`), a `vsnprintf` family, a failure path that flushes device `printf` and traps, a host-injected fixed clock for `today`/`now`, and the offline loopback network.
  - A concurrent program lowers through `compile_to_c_stepped`: the device lift places the stepped scheduler on the device, the host copies `LOGOS_SEED` into the device before the launch and reports a failed run after it, and the concurrency ops are in `codegen_cuda::CUDA_SCALAR_OPS`. `CudaLowerError::CRefused` carries the C tier's `#error` refusal in its words.
  - 112 of 117 ops lift; `Obs`, `CrdtJoin`, `BigIntWide`, `Tcp` and `Serve` are unreachable. `cuda_aot_lock`, `cuda_float_lock`, `cuda_failwith_trap_lock`, `cuda_temporal_lock`, `cuda_net_lock`; the GPU halves skip without `nvcc` and a device.
- **CUDA kernels from recognized tensor programs.** `compile_to_cuda_matmul`, `compile_to_cuda_matmul_with`, `compile_to_cuda_tensor_op` and `compile_to_cuda_gemm` lower a recognized matmul, elementwise or reduction program: a DP4A int8 GEMM anchored to `codegen_sva::systolic::eval_systolic` (`codegen_cuda::matrix`), and WMMA, cp.async multistage and `mma.sync` kernels chosen from a legality-checked tiling lattice (`codegen_cuda::gemm_tile::{CudaTile, cuda_tile_lattice, feasible_tiles, pareto_frontier, select_primitive, select_layout}`). `codegen_cuda::knobs::CudaKnobs` gives a knob only to a transform that wins in one regime and loses in another, each with its recorded trade-off (`STRICTLY_BETTER` lists the rest); `codegen_cuda::CudaChip` targets the RTX 3060 (sm_86) or the B200 (sm_100). `codegen_cuda::{emit_fused_row, emit_swiglu}` emit one-block-per-row RMSNorm, softmax and SwiGLU with a shared-memory tree reduction, and `compile_to_sol_solution` packages a recognized fused-row op as a SOL-ExecBench `solution.json` with a destination-passing torch binding. `cuda_matrix_lock`, `cuda_gemm_lattice_lock`, `cuda_gemm_tiled_lock`, `cuda_wmma_lock`, `cuda_knobs_lock`, `cuda_chip_lock`, `cuda_fused_row_lock`, `sol_solution_lock`.
- **Multi-stage forwards on CUDA, and transformer models authored in LOGOS.** `compile_to_cuda_forward` and its `_weighted`, `_q8`, `_q8_dp4a`, `_q8_moe`, `_q8_moe_dp4a` and `_q8_moe_exact` forms compose a program's ordered tensor-op stages into one CUDA program of chained parallel kernels over shared device buffers (`codegen_cuda::qwen_emit`): weights from a manifest as f32 or Q8_0-resident int8 with block scales, int8-activation DP4A matmuls, a two-pass activation mode, and grouped-expert MoE; `codegen_cuda::gguf::Gguf` reads GGUF metadata and Q8_0 tiles. `codegen_cuda::qwen38::gen_qwen38_lg` writes Qwen3.8-27B's hybrid forward (48 Gated DeltaNet and 16 gated-attention layers) as tensor-sugar LOGOS, with decode-step, prefill, batched and tensor-parallel generators, and `emit_qwen38_generate` lowers it to a CUDA generation program whose Gated DeltaNet kernel reads and writes each head's memory once per token (`codegen_cuda::gdn`). Its precise lane reproduces llama.cpp's greedy continuation token for token on the CPU reference and on an A100, recorded at 41.29 tok/s against llama.cpp's 42.17 (`benchmarks/results/qwen38_27b.json`). `cuda_forward_orchestrator_lock`, `cuda_full_forward_lock`, `cuda_forward_q8_lock`, `qwen38_golden_lock`, `qwen38_authored_lock`, `cuda_real_qwen8b_forward_lock`, `cuda_qwen30b_forward_lock`; the GPU legs skip without the weights, `nvcc` and a device.
- **An sm_86 SASS assembler core.** `codegen_sass` locates a kernel's machine code in a cubin, decodes each 128-bit instruction's control field (stall, yield, read/write barriers, wait mask, reuse) cross-checked against `nvdisasm`, re-encodes and patches it, builds dependency graphs, assigns stalls, list-schedules and optimizes operand reuse; `analyze_source` reports registers and occupancy for an emitted kernel. `sass_assembler_lock`, `sass_isa_lock`, `sass_depgraph_lock`, `cuda_sass_awareness_lock`.
- **Tenstorrent tier.** `codegen_tt::codegen_program_tt` and `compile::compile_to_tenstorrent` wrap the C tier's compute body as a TT-Metalium data-movement kernel on one Tensix baby core (`logos_compute_kernel.cpp`), with `Show` routed to DPRINT, a host orchestrator, CMake that requires `TT_METAL_HOME`, and the C oracle; channels, tasks, select, sleep and host sockets are refused with `TtLowerError::Unsupported` (a shrink-only ledger in `tenstorrent_op_coverage_lock`), and offline networking runs on-core. `compile_to_tenstorrent_matmul` and `compile_to_tenstorrent_tensor_op` recognize an affine triple-loop GEMM or an elementwise op and emit a tiled Tensix kernel from the program's dimensions (`codegen_tt::matmul_lower`); `compile_to_tenstorrent_{concurrency, fanout, select, racy_select, dynamic, multi_racy}` recognize pipe, fan-out and select programs, resolve every select arm at compile time from the source's virtual times, and print the multi-core program from the spatial IR (`codegen_tt::concurrency_mesh`). `tenstorrent_aot_lock`, `tt_matmul_lowering_lock`, `tt_concurrency_mesh_lock`, `tt_select_mesh_lock`, `tt_racy_select_lock`, `tt_dynamic_mesh_lock`, `tt_multi_racy_lock`; the on-card run is an `#[ignore]`d test.
- **Backend-neutral tensor recognition.** `tensor_recognize` lifts the parser's tensor sugar into typed specs (matmul, elementwise, reduction and fused-row, SwiGLU, fills), and `tensor_recognize::qwen` the transformer ops (rope, silu, sigmoid, gelu, decay, l2norm, transpose, row gathers, set-row, argmax, full, causal and grouped attention, causal conv, gated delta, MoE), each keyed on its own desugared shape; the CUDA, Tenstorrent and spatial front ends share them. `tensor_recognize_module_lock`, `tensor_op_coverage_lock`, `qwen_stage_coverage_lock`.
- **The spatial IR.** `spatial::ir::SpatialProgram` is one program every dataflow backend lowers from: load, store, receive, tile-compute and scalar stages over bounded buffers joined by typed handshakes (FIFO, semaphore pair, barrier, valid/ready, token), placed on a described mesh (`spatial::target::SpatialTarget::{wormhole, wormhole_ttsim, blackhole, sn40l_rdu, sn50_rdu, rtx3060}`) and scheduled as per-engine `ChoreoOp` programs.
  - Passes run in the order `spatial::pipeline::PASS_ORDER` pins: `tile` (a cost-model choice over the legal lattice; `Balanced` admits a ragged plan only where no evenly divided plan beats it on every predicted total, through `spatial::passes::tile::{admissible, best_among}`), `place`, `fuse_readers` (multicast), `size_buffers` (Little's-law depths under the memory budget), `synthesize_handshakes`, `persist` and `verify`, with `compose`, `pack`, `shard` and `fuse_generic` beside them.
  - `spatial::eval::eval_choreography` is the IR's executable meaning, and `spatial::equiv::equivalent_under` judges a rewrite over a seeded input corpus. Every verified program carries a `spatial::certificate::ChoreographyCertificate` (a marked-graph liveness witness, per-buffer balance and high-water bounds, a SAT certificate per integer PE datapath, the numerics claim and a sharded program's all-reduce obligation) whose `recheck` trusts only `logicaffeine_proof::pr` and integer arithmetic; `spatial::clock::cycle_period` is that graph's maximum cycle mean, and `spatial::work::work_of` counts the MVMULs, NOC transactions and bytes a schedule issues.
  - Backends are total (lower or refuse by name), and every emitted construct is the image of an IR object: `spatial::lower::tensix::TensixBackend` (reader, compute and writer kernels, circular buffers, runtime arguments and the host), `spatial::lower::cuda::CudaPersistentBackend` (a persistent sm_80+ kernel whose FIFOs are shared rings of `cp.async` commit groups, with `SpatialLowerError::TooBig` refusing a schedule that holds more pages of a ring than it has slots), `spatial::lower::fabric::FabricBackend` (synthesizable Verilog with a matching cycle model), `spatial::lower::rdu::RduSpatialBackend` (the certified neutral placement, `logos.spatial.neutral` v2, reading v1) and `spatial::lower::spatial_lang::emit_spatial_program` (Spatial source).
  - Measured: on Tenstorrent's simulator at 640³ on Wormhole, the compiler's own Tensix plan spends no more NOC bytes, NOC transactions or Tensix instructions than `ttnn.matmul` at matched numerics (`benchmarks/silicon/tenstorrent_gemm.json`, simulator counts, not silicon); on an RTX 3060 the persistent kernel reaches 17,994 GOPS at 2048³ bf16 against the tiling lattice's best 15,002 (`benchmarks/results/cuda_spatial_persistent.json`).
  - Tests: `spatial_ir_lock`, `spatial_choreography_semantics_lock`, `spatial_rewrite_lock`, `spatial_certificate_lock`, `spatial_clock_lock`, `spatial_work_model_lock`, `spatial_backend_totality_lock`, `spatial_one_ir_lock`, `spatial_tensix_lowering_lock`, `spatial_fabric_lowering_lock`, `spatial_rdu_lock`, `spatial_p6_ttsim_lock`, `cuda_spatial_persistent_lock`, `cuda_spatial_ring_lock`.
- **The SambaNova RDU backend and whole-model bundles.** `compile::compile_to_rdu` lowers a program through one of three lanes chosen by shape (`spatial::lower::rdu_lanes::lane_of`: tensor, counted loop, whole program) into an `RduArtifact`: the neutral placement, placement text, certificate, report and, where the lane has one, Spatial source; vendor configuration bytes stay gated on the SDK (`spatial::lower::rdu::GATED_REASON`).
  - `spatial::rdu_chip::RduChipFacts::{sn40l, sn50}` is the one source of chip facts, each labeled published or modeled. `spatial::lower::rdu_sdk::{RdaBridge, MockBridge, SambaFlowBridge}` ingest only an artifact whose certificate rechecks; `spatial::lower::sambaflow::emit_torch_module` writes the PyTorch module, weights manifest and compile scaffold SambaFlow ingests; `spatial::fit::{gemm_fit, qwen38_fit, vllm_plan, qwen38_serving_plan}` size a workload against the chip with no speed claim.
  - `compile::compile_qwen38_to_rdu_streamed` lowers Qwen3.8 to one certified program per layer kind, the embedding and head programs, and a manifest binding each layer's weights; `QwenDecode { ctx, batch, tp, prefill }` selects a decode step with a KV cache, a batched decode, a prefill chunk or a tensor-parallel decode (`spatial::tensor_parallel`: column and row splits with all-reduce sections). Q8_0 weights stay quantized on the chip, `spatial::residency` places activations and carried state in SRAM and plans what moves off chip, and `spatial::passes::pack` runs independent sections in one phase on disjoint sites. Executed layer by layer, the bundle emits the VM's token. Nothing ran on an RDU.
  - Tests: `rdu_one_lane_lock`, `rdu_loop_lane_lock`, `rdu_whole_program_lane_lock`, `rdu_sdk_bridge_lock`, `rdu_sambaflow_emit_lock`, `rdu_fit_report_lock`, `spatial_chip_facts_lock`, `qwen38_rdu_lock`, `qwen38_real_dims_lock`, `qwen38_decode_bundle_lock`, `qwen38_batched_decode_lock`, `qwen38_prefill_lock`, `qwen38_tensor_parallel_lock`, `qwen38_bundle_contract_lock`, `spatial_residency_lock`, `spatial_phase_packing_lock`.
- **A Plasticine (open RDU) lane, modeled end to end.** `compile::compile_to_plasticine` with `codegen_sva::{plasticine_report, spatial_emit}` turns a counted-loop LOGOS function or a tiled fp32 GEMM into Spatial source, a regression app that checks the VM's answer, a placement and a report row: per-workload PCU/PMU/FU utilization, every metapipeline stage's initiation interval with the critical stage flagged, N-buffer depths, hop counts and static-link occupancy, the DRAM roofline and GFLOPS against the published fp32 peak, anchored to the VM at the modeled II and SAT-certified for ALU-only bodies. `codegen_sva::plasticine_place` adds perimeter-hosted address-generator stages (`allocate_hosted`), FU-slot-aware PCU chaining, `place_unrolled`, `max_par` (PCU, PMU and perimeter-port capacity), `max_routable_par_within` and `place_routed`, which routes through a mesh router installed with `plasticine_place::install_mesh_router`; with none installed the placer routes X-then-Y. `PlasticineTarget` gains `pmu_vector_io` (ISCA'17 Table 3). Every row says `modeled`; nothing ran on Plasticine silicon. `plasticine_report_lock`, `metapipeline_lock`, `pmu_banking_lock`, `plasticine_place_lock`, `plasticine_sim_lock`.
- **The Silicon ISA listing and a portable binary bytecode.** `compile::compile_to_isa` prints the Oracle-optimized bytecode through `vm::isa::emit_isa`, tagging each instruction with its reversibility class; `vm::isa::{adiabatic_schedule, silicon_status, cell_class}` report charge recovery and each op's silicon home, and `Compiler::compile_for_chip` keeps `Div`/`Mod` for a chip with a divider. `vm::binisa::{encode_program, decode_program}` frame a whole `CompiledProgram` as a versioned, checksummed `LGB1` stream carrying its own string table, decoded into a fresh `Interner` to run identically; opcode numbers (`vm::binisa::ISA_OPCODES`) and builtin positions (`crates/logicaffeine_tests/tests/goldens/builtin_wire_ids.txt`) are append-only. `isa_aot_lock`, `builtin_wire_id_lock`.
- **Programs as circuits: the soft core, the fabric and the whole-program engine.**
  - `codegen_sva::full_asm::compile_to_full_words` assembles a program over the whole ISA (calls, division, heap) into the words `benchmarks/silicon/soft_core.v` executes, `codegen_sva::scalar_asm::compile_to_scalar_words` the function-free subset, and `codegen_sva::soft_core_model::run_soft_core_typed` is the cycle-accurate model both are held to (`soft_core_op_support_lock`, `universal_completeness_lock`, `nexys_bytecode_over_wire_lock`; `fpga_silicon_lock` runs on a Nexys A7-100T when a board is attached).
  - `codegen_sva::fabric_rtl` lowers a compiled residual to a fetch-free dataflow circuit (straight-line, if-converted decision, counted, while and nested loops, whole programs through `whole_program_fabric_from_source`) computing the VM's result modulo 2^64; `codegen_sva::fabric_tile` realizes 115 ops, each with an equivalence witness (`fabric_coverage_lock`). `codegen_sva::fabric_grid` maps a datapath onto a tile array with switchbox channels, SAT-certified equal to the datapath; `codegen_sva::fabric_target::{FabricTarget, place_with_target}` retargets it with typed `Unplaceable` refusals; `codegen_sva::pnr_certificate::PlacementCertificate::recheck` rechecks a placement with the PR/RUP checker alone; `codegen_sva::backend::TargetBackend` is the seam a hardware backend implements (`verified_placer_lock`, `fabric_target_lock`, `pnr_certificate_lock`, `target_backend_lock`).
  - `codegen_sva::wprog_engine_rtl::program_calls_fabric` flattens a program into a `WProgConfig` image and `eval_wprog_config` is its reference semantics; `emit_wprog_engine_soc_verilog` emits one netlist that receives the image and its parameters over a UART, runs recursion, heap collections, binary64 floats, text and Word32, and streams the result back, with a selectable memory backend and a frame-stack overflow trap. The core algorithms of the 35 benchmarks run equal to the VM on the engine model (`wprog_engine_soc_lock`, `wprog_completeness_lock`, `fabric_all_benchmarks_lock`, `wpe_backend_lock`, `wpe_depth_guard_lock`, `wpe_floorplan_lock`).
  - `codegen_sva::chip_protocol` defines the engine's UART protocol once: the program image's heap and go markers, the show, host-call and reply records, the result frame's sync byte and its end bytes (finished, call stack overflowed, heap overflowed), the value tags and the `HostCall` numbers. `Record`, `ChipValue` and `ResultFrame` encode and decode the framing and report a cut-off record as short, never as a value; `fill_verilog` fills the engine's Verilog templates, which name every protocol byte by placeholder; `table_json` hands the table to a host that is not Rust, and the `chip_host` example prints it for `scripts/logos-chip.py` (`chip_protocol_lock`).
- **Physical design as compiler output.** `codegen_sva::pdk_target::PdkTarget` (`SKY130A`, `GF180MCUC`) holds a process's cell library, metal stack with per-layer RC, SRAM macros, shuttle user area and harness timing, with typed `PdkUnsupported` refusals; `codegen_sva::flow_config::openlane_config` derives a block's OpenLane configuration from its `DesignProfile`, quoting the measurement behind each value; `codegen_sva::signoff_blocks::registered_period_ns` is the only period the emitter accepts for a registered block, derived from its slow corner; `codegen_sva::caravel_project` is the registry the Caravel scripts read; `codegen_sva::wpe_floorplan::plan_wpe` and `codegen_sva::shuttle_wrapper::plan_shuttle` plan memories and macros against the process. `codegen_sva::caravel_rtl::emit_dram_executor_wb_verilog` puts the certified DRAM command player behind a Wishbone slave with logic-analyzer observation inside the unmodified `user_project_wrapper`; its macro and wrapper are signoff-clean on sky130 and the assembled submission passes all 19 `mpw_precheck` checks (`benchmarks/silicon/mpw_precheck.json`). `flow_config_lock`, `signoff_period_lock`, `caravel_project_lock`, `sky130_provenance_lock`, `caravel_signoff_lock`, `mpw_precheck_lock`.
- **Coprocessor, memory and reversible-core RTL, each held to its software oracle.** `codegen_sva::{bignum_rtl, crdt_rtl, chan_rtl, arbiter_rtl, keccak_rtl, scratchpad_rtl, coproc_core, infinichip_rtl}` emit the BigInt datapath, the G-Counter merge, the bounded pipe FIFO, the SplitMix64 seeded draw, Keccak-f[1600] and the ML-KEM NTT butterfly, SECDED(72,64) and a double buffer, and a core that issues them; `codegen_sva::{systolic, simd, gpu_simt_rtl, dae, handshake_rtl}` a weight-stationary GEMM tile, SWAR lanes, a predicated SIMT engine, decoupled access-execute and the handshake primitives; `codegen_sva::{dram, ddr2_phy, computedram_rtl, storage_controller_rtl, memsched}` a DRAM command scheduler with an independent legality checker, a DDR2 controller and PHY, a ComputeDRAM device model, a zoned-NAND scheduler and a static memory-site classifier. Each is checked against its oracle under iverilog, with SAT-proven identities where they apply. `codegen_sva::rtl::{emit_reversible_alu_core_verilog, emit_isa_core_decoder_verilog}` emit the reversible ALU core and its opcode decoder, whose case labels come from `vm::binisa::opcode`, and `codegen_sva::reverse_module::discharge_reverse_module` proves a combinational unit reversible by exhaustive bit-blast, a GF(2) certificate and, under `verification`, Z3, failing if they disagree. `bigint_silicon_lock`, `crdt_silicon_lock`, `coproc_core_silicon_lock`, `weight_stationary_gemm_lock`, `pipelined_systolic_lock`, `simd_tile_lock`, `gpu_silicon_lock`, `dae_lock`, `ddr2_phy_sim_lock`, `computedram_sim_lock`, `storage_lock`, `memsched_lock`.
- **The analog lane, a mixed-signal partition and a DRAM compute-in-memory tier.** `compile::compile_to_analog` and `codegen_ana` lower a float function whose inputs carry `Assume x is between A and B.` ranges onto analog tiles (weighted Kirchhoff sums, programmed gains, four-quadrant multipliers) with a power-of-two gain schedule, a range certificate replayed by `logicaffeine_proof::interval_replay`, an itemized error budget whose `recheck` rederives its total, a placement, and a behavioral ngspice netlist with its simulator tolerances pinned. An integer region is exact when the budget proves ε below half a grid step; streamed reductions, OTA-C integrator loops, relaxation solves of `A·x = b` with an exact residual claim (`logicaffeine_proof::relax_replay`), a 2.5D/3D stack with a certified thermal map (`logicaffeine_proof::thermal_replay`) and per-die calibration build on the same artifact. Every refusal is a named `AnaLowerError`; every constant is sourced, derived or labeled modeled, and nothing is measured on analog silicon. `codegen_mx::partition` binds a function to the analog plane only when its certificate admits it and keeps its digital lowering in the same artifact; `codegen_dram` classifies every op for ComputeDRAM and composes bit-serial arithmetic, popcount, Hamming distance and set algebra from `{And, Or, Not}`. `analog_artifact_lock`, `analog_range_certificate_lock`, `analog_error_budget_lock`, `analog_int_lock`, `analog_reduction_lock`, `analog_relax_solve_lock`, `thermal_map_lock`, `mx_partition_lock`, `dram_op_coverage_lock`.
- **`aot_unsupported`: one refusal set for the single-device tiers.** `aot_unsupported::{Offload, classify, first, first_reachable}` classify once the operations a single-device backend cannot run (channels, tasks, virtual time, networking, the DOM); Tenstorrent, CUDA, analog and DRAM layer their own refusals on it, and `Offload::Dom` is how `compile_to_rust`, `compile_to_c` and `compile_to_cuda` refuse a program whose reachable code touches the DOM, naming it (`dom_tier_refusal_lock`).
- **More of the language on WebAssembly hosts.** New host imports `random_int`, `text_contains`, `logos_rt_fail`/`logos_rt_fail_at` (a runtime failure's message, worded through `semantics::failcode`, handed to the host before the trap) and `obs`; the linked runtime adds float-complex parts and `abs`, modular `÷` and `pow`, and `makeDate`/`makeMoment`, and the host shims carry the temporal components. `vm::wasm::{host_import_names, host_import_signatures, runtime_export_names, host_text_import_names, host_seq_import_names}` publish the append-only import catalog. A program's failure stops with the interpreter's reason on WebAssembly. `random_int_lock`, `wasm_temporal_host_lock`, `wasm_host_abi_lock`, `wasm_aot_lock`.
- **Pages compile to WebAssembly with a browser host.** A page (`## Page`/`## Component`, lowered to ordinary LOGOS by `logicaffeine_language`) compiles through `compile_to_wasm`; its DOM, event, route, fetch, storage and relay builtins lower to `env` imports that `crates/logicaffeine_compile/assets/web/runtime.js` (served as `web_host::BROWSER_RUNTIME_JS`) provides: the node table, the patch-stream applier, one guarded event entry, the fetch engine (request ids, abort, timeout), the relay connection, the router and the page clock, with a failure reported once per outermost entry in the compiler's own sentence. `web_host::{browser_host_html, browser_host_html_with_head, browser_host_html_themed, ShellTheme, with_csrf_meta, error_overlay_html, error_overlay_html_in, assemble_preview, wasm_host_shim, browser_host_js, DEFAULT_THEME_CSS, FAVICON_DATA_URI}` build the document, the themed shell (design tokens, element styles, dark mode, favicon), the build-error overlay and the Studio's live preview.
  - Every heap block carries its class and a count, a turn's garbage is freed at the safepoint after it, and the heap grows by `memory.grow` and fails with a named `OutOfMemory`; this is on for every page, and a linked program keeps the plain allocator (`wasm_heap_soak_lock`, `wasm_heap_class_lock`, `wasm_memory_bound_lock`).
  - `vm::reach::{code_layout, reach, running_ops, HostEntries}` compute what `Main` and the host's entries reach through calls, closures and spawned tasks; a page's module carries only the reconciler paths it can reach (`vdom_size_lock`, `vdom_calls_lock`, `wasm_host_entries_lock`).
  - Tests: `runtime_js_lock`, `fail_code_lock`, `wasm_dom_browser_lock`, `compile_web_theme_lock`, `hydration_lock`.
- **The page semantics every tier shares.** `semantics::dom` is the in-process DOM the tree-walker and the VM share, compared by node creation ordinal; `querySelector` walks the document from the body, so a node detached by a re-render never matches, and the HTML serializer escapes text and attribute values (`&`, `<`, `>`, and `"` in attributes) while `raw` content is emitted verbatim. `semantics::patch` is the length-prefixed patch stream with a strict decoder and an append-only opcode table; a stream carrying a retired opcode is refused whole. `semantics::storage` holds the `"local"` and `"session"` areas (a missing key is `None`, never `""`); `semantics::drive` is the one wording of a driving verb's outcome and the page clock; `semantics::world` the page world a run starts from; `semantics::failcode` the append-only failure codes a module reports. `vdom.lg` is the only reconciler: a page renders to a tape, a commit diffs it with keyed matching and minimal moves, a pre-rendered page is adopted rather than redrawn, and a server serializes `html of`, a `-> View` route, an error page and a page's `Head:` from the same tape. `dom_apply_lock`, `patch_stream_lock`, `patch_opcode_parity_lock`, `page_host_world_isolation_lock`, `vdom_lg_lock`, `retained_tree_lock`, `ssr_is_the_tape_lock`, `one_lgx_implementation_lock`.
- **Pages are tested on every tier they run on.** `page_host` drives a page's `## Test` over the page as the tree-walker runs it and as the VM runs it (`LoweredHost`, `LoweredPage`, `PageTier`), records per-statement traces, lets a host add a tier through `page_host::{RemotePage, RemotePageLauncher}`, and reads the shrink-only divergence ledger `crates/logicaffeine_tests/tests/lgx_known_divergences.txt` (`parse_ledger`, `stale_rows`); `vm::session::Session` holds a VM program open for host calls; `testrun::TierEvidence` reports a divergence as the first statement at which the documents differ. `page_trace_lock`, `lowered_vm_tier_lock`, `vm_session_lock`, `testrun_page_tiers_lock`.
- **`Serve` and the tcp.lg sockets run on the tree-walker, the VM and AOT-Rust.**
  - `Serve on port N.` and `Serve on port N with handler.` run an accept loop on the tree-walker and on the VM, where `Op::Serve` suspends into the async io driver and re-enters the handler as bytecode per connection (`Vm::call_handler_bytecode`); `Request`/`Response` carry a binary `bytes` body, a sanitized `contentType` and a header denylist, and `LOGOS_HOST`/`LOGOS_PORT` bind the address. The bound address is printed (`Serving on http://…`) when the port is bound, the signal a supervisor waits on. AOT-Rust lowers `Serve` onto `logicaffeine_system`'s HTTP server: `serve`, `serve_with_upgrades` when the pages use the relay, and `serve_here` when a handler reaches the program's bindings and must run on the thread that owns them (`route_aot_lock`).
  - Both loops carry the shared lifecycle from `LifecycleConfig::from_env`: bounded admission with a `503` shed, the configured body cap and read deadline on every read, opt-in readiness and liveness probes answered ahead of admission, and a graceful drain on `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT` that finishes in-flight work and releases the port. A read error answers by the shared `read_error_response` law on both engines, so an oversized declared body is `413` on each. A per-request wall-clock budget is checked at the per-statement checkpoint the op budget uses, sampled every 512 statements, and answered `503`; it bounds a runaway loop, not a runaway builtin.
  - A backend whose pages use the relay (a `shared on` store, a message trigger, a `Publish` in an event or action body, decided by `analysis::relay::{pages_use_the_relay, component_uses_the_relay, source_uses_the_relay}`) answers `/_relay` on every serving tier; an embedded site's `Serve` picks `serve_with_upgrades` from `logos_assets::USES_RELAY`.
  - `tcpConnect`, `tcpSend`, `tcpRecv`, `tcpRecvExact` and `tcpClose` run on both engines through one socket registry, each answering a `Result`; `Op::Tcp` is the VM's socket op; `ui_bridge::run_vm_io_async` drives both, with `run_vm_net_async` its alias.
  - Tests: `http_serve_cross_tier`, `tcp_cross_tier`, `tcp_natives`, `http_lifecycle_parity`, `backend_relay_lock`, `route_request_response_lock`, `serve_embed_assets_lock`.
- **`Swap`, `Reversibly:`, `Close`, `Await` and `Converge` lower on every executing tier.** `Swap x and y.` is `Op::RevSwap` on the VM and `std::mem::swap` on AOT-Rust. Inside `Reversibly:` (alias `Uncompute:`), `Set x to x + e`, `x - e`, `x xor e` and `-x` compile to `Op::RevAdd`/`RevSub`/`RevXor`/`RevNeg` with output identical to the plain lowering; the other tiers run the body as written. `Close ch.` and `Await h [into r].` are `ChanClose` and `TaskAwait` on the VM and scheduler-core operations on the C family. `Converge remote into local.` is the idempotent join `semantics::arith::crdt_join_field` (a bare `Int` field takes the MAX): `Op::CrdtJoin` on the VM, an inherent `join` on AOT-Rust. `converge_crdt_lock`, `rust_crdt_lock`, `universal_completeness_lock`, `op_backend_matrix_lock`.
- **A program stops with its own reason.** `Fail with <reason>.` (`BuiltinId::Fail`) and `Assert`/`Require`/`Assume that … , otherwise <reason>` (`BuiltinId::FailReason`, `semantics::builtins::contract_reason_text`) evaluate the reason only on failure, require it to be Text, and stop with the same words on the tree-walker, the VM, AOT-Rust, C, WebAssembly, the forge tier and the binary wire format. `error_fail_and_contracts`, `failure_all_tiers`, `backend_failure_matrix_lock`.
- **First-class tuples as parameter and return types.** `(Int, Text)` and a named record such as `(quotient: Int, remainder: Int)` type a parameter or a return value; `Let (q, r) be f()` destructures and `r's quotient` reads a field. AOT-Rust lowers a `LogosTuple` whose element types `item N of t` and field reads unbox to, and the VM builds a record when a named-record function returns a tuple literal. `param_return_tier_coverage_lock`.
- **CRDT kinds and `SharedMap`.** `semantics::crdt::CrdtKind` (`of_type_name`, `fresh`, stable codes, `lowered_by`, `refusal`) is the one table of CRDT kinds, and a backend that cannot lower one refuses it by name. `SharedMap from K to V` has add-wins keys and Lamport last-writer-wins values, with index read and write, `contains`, `Remove` and key-ordered iteration; `ConvergentCount`/`Tally` are per-replica state-based counters from their declaration (`Let n: Tally be 5.`) that show and compare as their totals; a CRDT crosses a task boundary as full state. `shared_map_all_tiers`, `crdt_backend_lock`, `shared_store_crdt_lock`.
- **New values and builtins.** `RuntimeValue::ComplexF`: `complex(re, im)` with a Real part builds it, `conjugate`, `re` and `im` are builtins, it displays as `2+i`, is unordered, and travels with wire tag 85; AOT-Rust carries it as `LogosComplexF`, including `Seq of ComplexF` and math.lg's `-> ComplexF` functions (`complex_seq_aot_lock`). `word8`/`intOfWord8` add `Word8`; same-width Words divide and take remainders with a zero check and compare unsigned; `splat4Word64` and `andNot4` run on the tree-walker and the VM as well as AOT-Rust. New builtins: `contains`, `makeDate`, `makeMoment`, `leading_zeros`, `trailing_zeros`, `readBytes`, `tryReadBytes`; `compressBytes`/`decompressBytes` (the smallest of deflate, lz4 and zstd, self-describing); `sealBytes`/`openBytes` and `sealFrame`/`openFrame` (compress, then ChaCha20-Poly1305, the tag verified before any byte is returned); `fromWireBytes`; `reedSolomonEncode`/`reedSolomonRecover` and `fecProtect`/`fecRestore` over GF(2^8). `BuiltinId::ALL`, `BuiltinId::effect()` (`BuiltinEffect::{Pure, Fallible, Nondeterministic, Effectful}`) and `BuiltinId::is_browser_only()` are exhaustive; builtin positions are append-only on the wire (`builtin_wire_id_lock`).
- **Tests are written in the language: `testrun`.** `testrun::{run_tests, run_tests_with_launcher}` take a `TestConfig` (filter, seed, op budget, live, prove, all tiers, page tiers) and run each `## Test` in a fresh interpreter in a sealed world: a virtual clock (`Advance time by …`), an in-memory file system (`Given a file "…" containing "…"`) and seeded randomness. `Expect that …` records and continues with power-assert rendering; `Expect this to fail [with "…"]:`, `Expect output:`, `Skip this test because "…"` and `This test may run up to N steps.` (exhaustion fails at the identical op) join it, and a test with no assertion is flagged vacuous. `For all integers|naturals|booleans x [where …]:` runs 256 SplitMix64 cases with shrinking, and prove mode exhausts boolean domains up to 16 variables. A run renders a name-ordered `RunReport` (`render_report`, `render_report_json`, `render_tier_summary`), an earned `Rank` (`render_fitness`) and a claim `Inventory` (`render_test_inventory_json`). `Interpreter` gains `enable_test_mode`, `set_tiers_noop`, `set_gen_seed`, `set_prove_mode`, `properties_proven`, `take_test_failures`, `test_ops` and `test_assertions`; `Compiler::compile_tiers` and `vm::run_tiers_strict` compile `Expect` and `ForAll` to no-ops so the VM runs a test's computation. `test_runner_lock`, `property_test_lock`, `rank_lock`, `inventory_lock`, `tiers_all_lock`, `hermeticity_redteam`.
- **The observation channel.** `Note/Warn/Record that <clause> [with a and b]`, `Note once in every N times that …` and `During <gerund>:` (balanced on every exit) emit structured events separate from program output. `observe::{ObsEvent, ObsCat, EventKind, install, drain, emit, enter_span, exit_span, capturing, to_logosobs, from_logosobs}` buffer them and read and write `.logosobs` (one JSON object per line). The tree-walker emits the stream; `Compiler::compile_observed` lowers to `Op::Obs` over `CompiledProgram::observation_sites` (`ObsSiteDesc`, `ObsSiteKind`) and `compile::vm_outcome_observed` runs it; `compile_to_rust_observed` and `codegen::with_observed_codegen` call `logicaffeine_system::obs`; `compile_to_wasm_observed` reports sites through an `obs` import. The release profile erases observation before the optimizer (`elide_observation`), so the emitted Rust is byte-identical to the program without it. `obs_emission_lock`, `obs_span_balance_lock`, `obs_channel_separation_lock`, `obs_vm_emission_lock`, `obs_aot_emission_lock`, `obs_wasm_lock`, `obs_wire_lock`, `obs_zero_cost_codegen_lock`.
- **`replay`: a failing run is a replayable bundle.** `replay::ReplayBundle` (`REPLAY_FORMAT` = 1) carries the embedded source and its FNV-1a hash, the seed, the scheduling trace, the transcript and a failure cursor classified by `FailureKind::{RuntimeError, Assertion, Deadlock, TestFailure}`; `capture_failure` records on the seeded concurrent VM (`ui_bridge::run_vm_concurrent_traced`), and `verify` answers `Verify::{Reproduced, Diverged, NoFailure}`. `replay_lock`.
- **A debugger facade, and a VM that runs backward.** `debug_session::DebugSession` (`launch`, `threads`, `stack_trace`, `variables`, `next`, `step_in`, `step_out`, `continue_`, `step_back`, `reverse_continue`, breakpoints, `evaluate`, with `DapThread`/`DapFrame`/`DapVariable`) is the one facade `largo dap` and the Studio share; `frame_eval::{frame_eval, frame_answer, Binding}` evaluate an expression, or answer an `Is X <predicate>?` question with `Yes.`/`No.`, against paused bindings. `Vm::run_recording` (and `_mode`, `_budgeted`, `_driven`) journals only what each op destroys onto a `vm::journal::HistoryTape`, and `Vm::run_reverse` replays the executed trace backward to the exact pre-run state; `vm::reverse::reversibility` classifies every `Op` as `RevClass::{Bijection, Journaled, Effectful, Control}` with no wildcard arm; `vm::pebble::{plan, plan_budgeted, report}` elide or recompute scratch no reverse step reads (`ReverseMode::{FullState, Observable, Adiabatic}`, `Vm::recompute_ops`). An op outside the reversible subset is declined before the run; spawned tasks, channels, `Await` and `Select` reverse schedule-backward (`compile::program_outcome_scheduled`). `dap_lock`, `session_parity_lock`, `frame_eval_lock`, `reverse_op_lock`.
- **Strict engines, engine fuel, a tier differential and bytecode-IR entry points.**
  - `EngineId`, `EngineOutcome { engine_ran, output, error }`, `run_strict_tw[_with_args]` and `run_strict_vm[_with_args]` (re-exported at the crate root): a program the VM cannot run is a typed refusal, never a silent tree-walker run; `LOGOS_ENGINE_TRACE` names the engine on stderr (`strict_engine_lock`).
  - `compile::tier_differential` runs the tree-walker and the VM and answers `TierVerdict::Agree`, or `Divergence { compiler_bug: true, bundle }` with a ready-to-file reproduction.
  - `compile_to_program` (the canonical, wasm32-buildable `source → CompiledProgram`), `compile_to_program_optimized` (prelude, decorators, division resolution and the AOT optimizer), `program_outcome`, `program_outcome_scheduled`, `program_outcome_concurrent`, `interpret_bootstrap` and `FIRST_CLASS_VALUE_KINDS`; `Backend` with `refuse_unlinkable[_with]` refuses a reachable native kernel or an unlowered CRDT kind by name on C, RISC-V, WebAssembly and the soft core (`backend_execution_diff_lock`, `crdt_backend_lock`, `native_kernel_reach_lock`, `bootstrap_scope_lock`).
  - A run failure names where it happened: `tw_outcome_located` and `vm_outcome_located` prefix it with `line:col — <site>`; `CompiledProgram::site_at`, `NO_SITE`, `Vm::failing_site` and `vm::run_to_outcome_located` carry the site on the VM, and `vm::compiler::Refusal { at }` with `compile_with_oracle_located`/`compile_observed_located` locate a compile refusal (`runtime_failure_location_lock`, `vm_failure_site_lock`, `refusal_span_lock`).
  - `interpreter::{install_fuel, clear_fuel, active_fuel, FuelHandles}` arm one deterministic step budget across every tree-walker task and the VM; a budgeted VM run stays on the dispatch loop, so tiered native code cannot escape the count. `fuel_budget`, `run_limits`.
  - VM API: `Op::name` and `Op::all_variants`; `vm::{run_to_outcome_observed, run_nanos}` and `interpreter::interp_nanos` (run-only timing); `BoundaryType::{Result, Set, Option}`; `CompiledProgram::declared_types`; `vm::SOME_BINDING_MARKER`; `Op::for_each_written_reg`/`Op::for_each_read_reg`, exhaustive, answer which registers an op writes and reads for every lowering that tracks registers.
- **`ui_bridge` seams and theorem certificates.** `ui_bridge::theorem_certificate` builds a kernel `TheoremCertificate` (proof term, goal, disclosed premises) for a kernel-proved `## Theorem`, proved by its declared `Proof:` method (`proof_certificate_lock`); `TheoremCompileResult::discharged` counts the obligations automation closed behind a declared method and `TheoryCompileResult::axiom_names` lists admitted axioms. `with_parsed_program_checked`/`with_monomorphic_program_checked` enforce `## Require` and answer in author coordinates; `with_parsed_tensor_program`, `parse_error_of`, `failure_sites_of`, `lgx_findings_of` (a page's structural and accessibility findings), `value_copy_findings_of` and `program_required_opts` serve the Studio and editors.
- **Exact 64-bit integer and binary64 arithmetic on narrow device lanes (`accel`).**
  - `codegen_sva::fabric_rtl::Divergence` and `AluOp`/`FuncUnit::eval_checked` reconcile the fabric's fixed-width
    cells with the VM's kernel: a result that promotes past `i64`, a zero divisor, a refused exponent and a float
    division by zero are named instead of wrapped, zeroed or saturated (`fabric_checked_semantics_lock`).
  - `accel::lane` is a device's integer datapath at its declared word and multiplier widths: lane routines,
    their interpreter, and their costs. It refuses any unit the shape lacks (`accel_lane_machine_lock`).
  - Built from lane operations and parameterized by the shape:
    - `accel::multiword`: the multi-word integer core (`accel_multiword_lock`);
    - `accel::int64`: the VM's checked 64-bit integer operations;
    - `accel::softfloat`: IEEE-754 binary64 with round-to-nearest-even, subnormals, signed zeros and the x86 NaN
      rule.
  - `accel::cells` makes every fabric cell a lane routine whose result is `eval_checked`'s, with the divergence
    code in a fault word, at 2- to 32-bit lanes (`accel_int_routines_lock`, `accel_softfloat_lock`).
- **One source for what a program reports.** `compile::{tw_lines, tw_lines_with_args, vm_lines_net}` keep a run's shown lines apart, so standard output can be compared byte for byte with a compiled binary's; `semantics::policy::{NO_POLICIES, no_predicate, check_failed}` word a `Check` the same on the tree-walker and the VM; `semantics::escape::REFUSAL` is the engines' one refusal of an `Escape` block; `semantics::failcode::js_cases` renders the WebAssembly Node host's failure sentences from `failcode::message`, so the host cannot word a failure differently from the reference tier.
- **The bytecode→C walker runs every program the VM runs, byte for byte: stdout, stderr and exit status.**
  - Failure sentences are the VM's own, taken at generation time from the functions the VM calls (`codegen_c::words`): division, remainder, floor and exact division by zero, negative and oversized exponents, indices, missing keys, invalid UTF-8, `parseInt`, `chr`, file reads, the network's `Await` without `Listen` and bad addresses.
  - Lowered natively: `today`/`now`, `args()`, `randomInt`/`randomFloat` (the VM's uniform draw and crossed-bound refusal), `text_bytes`/`text_from_bytes` with Rust's UTF-8 validation, word arithmetic and bit counts, `tryReadBytes`, the sandboxed file system, structural equality and display of every value, undeclared enums (`Option`, `Result`) with program-wide constructor tags, sequences of any element kind, and a native the program declares and no body implements (it gives `nothing`, as on the VM).
  - Value semantics through owned flags: a change copies a collection only while another name may hold it (copy on write without reference counts), and `text + tail` appends in place to a text the register owns, so building a text is linear.
  - The offline peer network carries typed messages and validates addresses as the VM does; `loader::keep_reachable_functions(user_source, spliced)` seeds the functions a `## Route` dispatcher and a derived codec call, which the compiler writes and no scan of the source sees.
  - **Exact integers on machine words.** `vm::exactness` finds every integer the program can carry past i64 (one decision for every backend that lowers the bytecode); the walker emits such a region twice, on machine words and held exact (a tagged word: small values shifted, big ones a boxed `LInt`), and moves into the exact version, with every live value, at the first operation or arriving value that does not fit. A check with a constant operand is a range test beside the value path (`3 * k + 1` stays one `lea`); any other is a predicted-not-taken overflow branch. `25!`, `2^62 * 4`, Fibonacci past `2^63` and exact parameters, results and globals print what the VM prints; collatz runs at 0.955-0.996x of the unchecked code.
  - **The offload seam** for the accelerator backends: `codegen_c::from_program::{Offload, OffloadedC, compile_program_to_c_offloaded, compile_program_to_c_with_offloads}` and `compile::compile_to_c_via_bytecode_offloaded`. A device may answer an offloaded function's calls at its entry (`logos_dev_call`); a declined call, or an exact argument past i64, runs the host body; a port a parameter's settled kind does not fit keeps the function on the host, reported with both kinds; an empty table emits no trace of the seam.
  - `optimize::memoize_for_bytecode`: `## Require memo` holds on the bytecode backends as on the run path.
  - One source for what the tree-walker, the VM and the C runtime share: `concurrency::net_inbox::{AWAIT_WITHOUT_LISTEN, peer_topic, relay_url}` and `semantics::builtins::{vfs_read_failure, vfs_write_failure}`. The VM's sandboxed file builtins no longer block on the asynchronous file system, so they no longer panic under a running executor.
- **`model::tokenizer`: a checkpoint's vocabulary, as llama.cpp tokenizes.** `Vocab::from_gguf` reads SentencePiece and byte-level BPE vocabularies with every pre-tokenizer llama.cpp names (`tokenizer.ggml.pre`), and `tokenize`, `token_to_piece` and `detokenize` follow llama.cpp's own code: its regex splits run on a port of libstdc++'s ECMAScript backtracking engine over llama.cpp's collapsed categories, and its special tokens are ordered and its end-of-turn token chosen through ports of libstdc++'s `std::sort`, `_Hash_bytes` and `unordered_map` iteration order. The Unicode tables are generated from llama.cpp's `unicode.cpp` (`model_tokenizer_lock`).
- **The `f64` reference reads every architecture the spec does.** `ModelSpec::from_gguf` and `model::reference` add Gated DeltaNet with gated attention and multi-section rotation (Qwen3.5), sliding windows, sandwich norms, an embedding scale and final soft-cap (Gemma 3), attention sinks, projection biases, the clamped SwiGLU and top-k-then-softmax routing (gpt-oss), YaRN with its magnitude, and multi-head latent attention with sigmoid or grouped routing, shared experts and dense lead layers (DeepSeek). `Reference::step_every` returns the logits after every token, and `Reference::closest_routing` names the narrowest expert choice a run made (`model_reference_lock`, `model_conformance_lock`).
- **`model::gguf::write`** writes a GGUF v3 checkpoint, metadata in order and each tensor aligned, refusing bytes that do not fit a tensor's type; `Gguf::data_start` and `Gguf::header_digest` locate and identify a file's data (`model_gguf_lock`).
- **`codegen_cuda::lane`: generation compiled from the authored step.** `lane::compile` takes a spec, a checkpoint and `LaneOptions` (context, knobs, prefill chunk). `author::decode_step` and `author::prefill` write the step and a chunk of a prompt, the shared tensor front end lifts each into a `StepGraph` (token and position as parameters, caches, rings and memories as carried state), `weights::place` reads each Q8_0 projection as rows in place from the checkpoint, and `plan` lowers each op to the kernel library with the `add_norm`, `gemv_bias`, `head_norm_rope`, `slice_read` (a part of every head read where it lies by the norm, rotation or per-head product that reads it), `row_write`, `join_in_place` (a join's two halves written by their producers straight into the joined tensor, or into the cache row the join lands in), `conv_silu`, `conv_silu_l2`, `gates`, `act_gate`, `gather_scale`, `strided_values`, `argmax_advance` and `fused_quant` fusions, each legal by dataflow alone; a projection reading one row of a block reads that row of the block's quantized form in place. `emit` prints one CUDA program holding the decode step and a form for every power-of-two chunk up to the prefill size (512 at most), sharing constants and carried state: a chunk of more than sixteen rows multiplies on the int8 tensor cores (`mma.sync` m16n8k32, double-buffered `cp.async`), and every form is captured as a CUDA graph (`cuda_graphs`). The program checks it reads the checkpoint it was compiled against and generates with the token and position on the device; `--prompts FILE` runs one prompt a line, the carried state starting over for each, and `--profile` launches the decode step without its graph and prints each launch's kind, result and mean time. What it cannot compile is refused by name (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **DeepSeek's latent attention through the front end, the spatial IR and the lane.** `HeadSliceSpec`, `JoinSpec` and `HeadMatmulSpec` (`recognize_head_slice`, `recognize_join`, `recognize_head_matmul`, `TensorOpSpec::{HeadSlice, Join, HeadMatmul}`) lift the head forms; `AttentionSpec::{vd, scale}` carries an attention's value width and a given scale, and `MoeSpec::renormalize` a mixture's weighting. The spatial IR lowers the three forms as banded flat sections (`from_head_slice`, `from_join`, `from_head_matmul`) and an attention's narrower values and given scale through its three-section form; the flash form keeps values as wide as keys (`attention_reads_dense` takes the value width). The spatial IR and the single-token CUDA forward refuse an un-renormalized mixture by name, and the forward refuses narrower values and a given scale. In the lane, `lk_slice`, `lk_join`, and the per-head `lk_head_gemv` (a head's checkpoint rows are its outputs) and `lk_head_axpy` (its inputs) read any weight format in place under every activation precision; the attention kernel takes a value width, a value row stride and offset, and its scale; the `strided_values` fusion lets an attention read its values in place in the latent cache; the router takes a renormalization flag. `Launch::projected_input` names the activation every product by checkpoint rows reads quantized. `author` writes multi-head latent attention absorbed (each head's query through the key half of the expansion, one cache row of latent and shared rotated key a position, the attended latent through the value half) for the forward, the decode step, the prefill chunk and the batch, with a low-rank query, shared experts and routing without renormalization. `bind` binds the query, the latent projection and norm, both halves of `attn_kv_b` or `attn_k_b`/`attn_v_b`, and the shared experts (`View::NeoxPairs::from`, `View::HeadPart::transpose`, `View::HeadBlocks`); `Mla::kv_b_split` records the split, and the rotation helpers count a latent attention's rotation. `reference` computes latent attention absorbed, quantizing its activations where the lane does.
- **The lane's attention, split over the span.** `kernels::ATTENTION` cuts each query row's span into chunks fixed by the row's length alone (`ATTN_MIN_SPLIT`, at most `attn_splits` of them): the decode step runs a chunk a block and the last block to arrive folds the chunks in order, and a prefill chunk whose rows already fill the chip runs every chunk of a row in the row's block in the same arithmetic, so a prompt continues alike token by token or in chunks of any size. A block takes the query heads of one key/value group whose keys and values a position stay within `ATTN_BLOCK_VALUES` (`attn_heads_per_block`), a warp scores several keys at once, and keys and values are read four and two values at a time; `plan::Launch::Attention::{per_block, splits, parallel}` carry the choice and `CudaChip::sms` the streaming multiprocessors it fills. A cache's length is bounded by memory alone, and the refusal moves to a head wider than one block's shared memory or one the vector loads cannot read. Under the precise numerics every sum of the attention — scores, softmax, values, chunk partials and their fold — runs in `f64` (`attn_accumulator`), as the lane's other sums do. A literal row of a carried table written in place before the row's last reader is copied where the program takes it, not viewed (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **The gated pair in one launch, the router across the chip.** The `glu_pair` fusion folds two projections of one activation and the gate between them — SwiGLU, or a GeLU or SiLU gate inside its product — into one launch (`plan::Launch::GemvGlu`, `kernels::GLU`'s `lk_gemv_glu`): a warp an output row dots both rows with the dot the plain GEMV of each format takes (the Q8_0 row dot now `lk_q8_row_dot`, shared with `lk_gemv_q8`), so the fused and unfused programs agree bit for bit, and a quantized output is quantized in its epilogue, a block of 32 outputs a launch block. It fires where the pair runs as GEMVs; a chunk the tensor cores multiply keeps its GEMM. The expert router computes its logits a warp an expert across the grid's blocks, and the last block to arrive takes the softmax and the top `K`, each pick a warp-wide arg-max with the scan's tie rule; a router past the default shared memory has the attribute set on its own instantiation (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **The shared expert inside its mixture's launches, and every fusion checked against its parts.** The `shared_expert` fusion folds a mixture's shared SwiGLU expert into the mixture's launches when nothing reads its parts except its sum with the mixture (`plan::SharedExperts`): `lk_moe_up` computes its gate and up rows after the kept experts' rows, the hidden rows are quantized together, and `lk_moe_down` gives it one warp of the block beside the kept experts' warps, added after their sum. Every dot is the dot the plain GEMV of its format takes. `kernels::BLOCKS`' `lk_gemv_dot` is now the one row dot of each format every kernel shares, and the mixture's own Q8_0 dot is gone. So the folded program computes what the shared expert's own launches computed. The fusion rules form a table (`plan::RULES`) that a plan can hold back (`lane::compile_holding`), producing the program each fusion is checked against (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **Several rows a warp, a norm in registers, a router across the chip.** A projection, a gated pair or a mixture with at least 2048 output rows of at least 64 sub-blocks in a block format, at one activation row, now takes four output rows a warp (`plan::RULES`' `rows_a_warp`, `kernels::rows_a_warp`, `Launch::{Gemv, GemvGlu, Moe}::per_warp`). The rows share the activation's loads and each row's dot is unchanged (`lk_row_dot<F, Q, M, W>`, one sub-block dot `lk_sub_dot`). A projection of four rows a warp that a projection reads quantized does the quantization in its epilogue, 32 outputs a block (`Launch::Gemv::{quant, f32}`). The quantizing epilogue of the gated pair takes 256 threads. An RMSNorm reads its row once and keeps it in registers across up to 1024 threads (`kernels::norm_threads`); a norm inside a rotation takes the same threads, so it sums a head's squares as the norm kernel does. An `f32` expert router reads each expert's row with four warps, 16 bytes a lane (`kernels::route_warps`). `examples/model_lane --hold=<rule>,…` compiles a checkpoint's lane with rules held back, to measure each rule.
- **The latent absorption staged in shared memory, the arg-max across the chip.** Under the quantized numerics, a per-head matmul whose rows are its inputs now stages them in shared memory (`kernels::HEADS`' `lk_head_axpy_staged`, chosen by `kernels::axpy_staged`). Each row's units are read in 16-byte loads from its aligned-down start. A lane decodes its rows' sub-blocks whole, and a transposing butterfly (`lk_fly`) sums the warp's rows. On the 3060 this runs 1.7–2.8 times faster than the per-lane kernel in Q4_K, Q5_K and the legacy formats; DeepSeek-V2-Lite's absorption goes from 19.9 µs to 9.7. The per-lane kernel keeps the precise numerics, Q6_K and Q8_0, every form alike. A matrix in a block format is uploaded with 32 zero bytes past its last row. The arg-max now spreads over up to 64 blocks, and the last block to arrive takes the first maximum over them (`kernels::argmax_blocks`).
- **A block's expert mixtures in one launch set.** A prefill chunk's mixtures, one for each row of a stacked tensor, each writing the same row of a table, now fold into one mixture over the rows (`plan::RULES`' `moe_rows`, `Launch::Moe::rows`). Each row is routed on its own (`lk_moe_route`, a grid row a row). The rows' choices are grouped by expert in row-then-choice order (`lk_moe_group`). Each choice's activation row is gathered (`lk_moe_gather`). Each expert runs its gate, up and down rows over its choices' rows in slabs of 16, 8, 4, 2 and 1 (`lk_moe_up_rows`, `lk_moe_down_rows`, `lk_slabs`), so its weights are read once for every row that chose it. The rows' weighed results are summed in choice order (`lk_moe_combine`). Every row's dots are those of one row, so the fold computes, bit for bit, what the per-row mixtures computed. DeepSeek-V2-Lite now prefills a 128-token prompt at 304 tokens a second on the 3060, up from 210.
- **Every sum of the precise lane in `f64`.** Under the precise numerics, these now sum in `f64` (`kernels::accumulator`), as the projections and the attention already did: the norms (`lk_rmsnorm`, the norm inside a rotation, `lk_l2norm`), the convolution's taps and its L2 norm, the delta rule's three dots and its output norm, the router's softmax and its renormalization, and the experts' weighted sum. At a width of 2048 the precise lane had drifted 1.4e-5 of the logits' scale from the reference through its `f32` sums. The quantized numerics compute exactly what they computed before (`cuda_lane_lock`).
- **Wide chunks on the int8 tensor cores in every block format, their experts too.** Under the quantized numerics a chunk of more than sixteen rows now multiplies its projections on the int8 tensor cores in any block format (`kernels::GEMM_BLK`'s `lk_gemm_blk`, `Kernel::GemmBlk`), as a Q8_0 chunk already did; the `cp.async` and `mma` helpers both GEMMs share are `Kernel::Mma`. A block computes a 64 × 128 tile over stages of 128 `k`: the activation rows land in shared memory by `cp.async`, each weight row's sub-blocks are decoded into int8 levels with each half's scale and offset (`lk_sub4`), and each half's activation sum is taken for the offsets. A warp multiplies 32 × 32, one `m16n8k32` a sub-block, or two `m16n8k16` in a format whose halves scale apart (Q2_K, Q3_K, Q6_K). A gated pair such a chunk multiplies stays two GEMMs (`glu_pair` holds back). A wide chunk's expert mixture multiplies each expert's matrices the same way, a grid layer an expert (`lk_gemm_blk`'s grouped mode, a Q8_0 expert read as its levels and one scale a sub-block): gate and up over the gathered rows of the expert's choices, the gated activation (`lk_moe_glu`), and down with each choice's row weighed by its weight. Such a chunk sums in the `mma`'s order, so it is held to the reference rather than to the token-at-a-time run bit for bit; the precise numerics keep the GEMV's slabs and their bit-for-bit continuation. DeepSeek-V2-Lite on the 3060 now prefills a 128-token prompt in chunks of 64 at 658 tokens a second, up from 304 (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **One arena for every step's own tensors.** `lane::arena` places every array a step computes and reads within the step — a tensor's `f32` buffer or its quantized form, all written before any launch reads it, whole or as parts of every unit that together cover each unit — by its lifetime, from its first touch to its last through any view of it, the largest first at the lowest offset clear of every array live beside it (`arena::place`, `Form::arena`, `Launch::{writes_whole, writes_part}`). Every launch's scratch lives there too while the launch runs (`arena::scratch`, a launch's own rather than one its shape shares); its arrival counts, zero before the launch and after it, keep zeroed buffers of their own. The program allocates one arena, the largest form's, since one form runs at a time, and fills it with `0xff`, so a read of a byte no launch of the step wrote is a NaN. Arrays a step reads before writing them, carried state, constants, inputs and outputs keep their own buffers. `Launch::on_tensor_cores` is the one rule the arena and the emitter read. DeepSeek-V2-Lite's step-local tensors across its forms up to a 512-row chunk took 4,970 MiB, past the 3060's memory beside the weights; they and every launch's scratch now take one arena of 94.2 MiB (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **A wide chunk's attention and per-head products in tiles.** Under the quantized numerics, a chunk of more than sixteen rows now attends in tiles (`kernels::ATTENTION_TILED`'s `lk_attention_tiled`, `Kernel::AttentionTiled`, chosen by `Launch::attends_in_tiles`). A block takes 32 query vectors — `kernels::attn_tile_heads` heads of one key/value group over consecutive rows — and walks the keys its rows attend 64 at a time. It scores each tile in 32-wide chunks of the head staged in shared memory, takes each query's running maximum and sum, and weighs the tile's values in registers, so every key and value it loads serves all 32 queries. Windows, sinks, grouped heads and values read in place in the cache are kept. Its per-head matmuls run in tiles too (`kernels::HEAD_TILED`'s `lk_head_tiled`, `Kernel::HeadTiled`, `Launch::heads_in_tiles`), with matrix rows as outputs or as inputs: 64 rows by 64 outputs a block, each weight dequantized as the CPU dequantizer does and each input read as its quantized words times their scale. Both sum in `f32` as the kernels they replace there do. The 16-row chunk, the decode step and the precise numerics keep their kernels, and so their bit-for-bit continuation. DeepSeek-V2-Lite's latent attention over a 512-token chunk drops from 117 ms to 42.6 and its per-head products from 64 ms to 30; a 512-token prompt in one chunk prefills at 1,825 tokens a second on the 3060, up from 1,209 (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **A wide chunk's router as one GEMM, its choices grouped in parallel, and a taller block GEMM.** Under the quantized numerics, a wide chunk's expert mixtures route their rows by one GEMM of logits — `kernels::GEMM_F32`'s `lk_gemm_f32` (`Kernel::GemmF32`) for an `f32` router, the block GEMM for a router in a quantized format — and a pick a row (`lk_moe_pick`), whose softmax and top `K` are the routine `lk_moe_pick_row` that `lk_moe_route` shares. `lk_moe_group` now groups the choices in parallel and in the serial order: counts by shared atomics, then a block-width of choices at a time, each placed after its expert's earlier choices in earlier rounds, warps and lanes (`__match_any_sync`; `kernels::moe_group_threads`, `kernels::moe_group_smem_bytes`). The block GEMM's tile is `BM` activation rows, a template parameter: `kernels::GEMM_BLK_TILE_M` (128) for a dense product, and 64 or 128 for an expert's rows by `kernels::gemm_blk_expert_rows`, with `BM · 4` threads (`kernels::gemm_blk_threads`). Its shared memory holds only the scales, offsets and 16-bit half sums its format needs (`kernels::gemm_blk_smem_bytes(format, two, rows)`, `lk_stage_acts`, `lk_stage_rest`); each row's activation scales are staged beside its tile, and each sub-block's dot is scaled as `(s·d)·dot + o·(s·Σ)`. Over DeepSeek-V2-Lite's 512-token chunk the router drops from 11.4 ms to 4.1 and the grouping from 4.5 ms to 0.2; the prompt prefills at 1,927 tokens a second, up from 1,825 (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **The block GEMM's next stage in flight while this one multiplies.** Where two stages of activations fit a block's 99 KB of shared memory (`lk_stage_pipelined`), the next stage's activation rows land by `cp.async` in the other buffer while this one multiplies. Each thread's weight sub-blocks for the next stage are fetched before this stage's barriers and multiply (`lk_raw<F>`, `lk_fetch`, `lk_stage_fetch`), as the bytes `lk_sub4` reads in the widest loads the format's alignment allows: 16 bytes in Q4_K, Q5_K and Q8_0's levels, 4 in Q4_1, Q5_1 and Q2_K, 2 in Q4_0, Q5_0, Q3_K and Q6_K, 1 in MXFP4. They are decoded from those words (`lk_unpack`) operation for operation as `lk_sub4` decodes them, so every product is unchanged bit for bit; a block format with no raw words is a compile error. Over DeepSeek-V2-Lite's 512-token chunk the expert GEMMs drop from 1,586 µs to 1,320 (gate and up) and from 1,262 to 1,207 (down), and the prompt prefills at 2,112 tokens a second, up from 1,927 (`cuda_lane_lock`).
- **The latent's expansion halves read their activations exactly; a wide chunk attends expanded.** Under every numerics the per-head products — the key half that takes a head's unrotated query into the latent's space, the value half that takes its attended latent out — read their activations `f32`, summing in `f64` under the precise numerics and in `f32` under the quantized ones (`kernels::head_mode`, the kernels' `Q` 3: `lk_f32_acts`, `lk_row_dot_f32`, a lane half a sub-block, its levels a word and its inputs sixteen bytes at a time). The reference reads them unquantized too, so the absorbed and the expanded attention take the same scores and values. Under the quantized numerics a chunk of more than sixteen rows now attends expanded (`plan`'s `latent_expanded`): each cached latent row before the chunk's last position runs through both halves (`Launch::HeadGemv::{shared, upto}` — every head reading the row's one latent, the rows bounded at run time — `lk_head_tiled`'s row pitch and position bound), and each head's query attends its own keys and values (`plan::Split`, `lk_attention_tiled`'s two-part query and key): the query its unrotated part read in place in the query projection, then its rotated part; the key its expansion, then the rotated key every head shares, read in place in the cache. The decode step and the narrow chunks keep the absorbed attention over a cache `heads·(qk_nope + v_dim)/(kv_lora_rank + qk_rope)` times smaller. `lk_head_tiled` stages its weights from their raw words fetched a stage ahead (`lk_fetch`, `lk_unpack`, now in `kernels::BLOCKS`), bit for bit what it staged before. A rotation's result is `heads · hd` values, however many rows it turns (`lane::graph`). Over DeepSeek-V2-Lite's 512-token chunk the latent attention drops from 73.4 ms to 32.3, and the prompt prefills at 2,565 tokens a second, up from 2,112; decode holds at 136.6 (`model_reference_lock`, `lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **An expert's gate and up in one GEMM, its hidden rows quantized in the epilogue.** Where a wide chunk's mixture keeps its gate and up in one format, `lk_gemm_blk`'s paired mode (`G` 3, `Launch::pairs_gate_and_up`) multiplies both in one launch: a block's weight rows are an expert's 64 gate rows and the same 64 up rows (`lk_tile_row`, `lk_stage_fetch` and `lk_stage_weights` taking the up matrix for the tile's second half), and its epilogue adds each projection's bias, takes the gated activation as `lk_moe_glu` does (SwiGLU, or the clamped gate under `ACT` 1) and quantizes each 32-value block as `lk_quant_lane` does — the hidden rows' words and scales byte for byte what the two GEMMs, `lk_moe_glu` and `lk_quant` wrote, with no `f32` gate, up or hidden rows (`arena::scratch`). A gate and an up in two formats keep the four launches. A warp whose 32 rows all lie past its tile's last row — an expert with few rows — leaves the tile's multiply to the others, its results unchanged bit for bit. Over DeepSeek-V2-Lite's 512-token chunk the experts' gate and up drop from 75.7 ms to 61.6 and their down GEMMs from 31.6 ms to 28.3, and the prompt prefills at 2,786 tokens a second, up from 2,565 (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **A dense GEMM's last wave filled, the router across the chip, the per-head tiles and the attention fed ahead.** A tensor-core projection whose tiles leave the chip's last wave part empty runs on a line of blocks (`Launch::Gemv::tail`, `kernels::gemm_blk_tail`): a block each for the tiles of the whole waves, and each remaining tile cut along `k` into as many parts as fill the last wave, each part parking its sums and the tile's last part to arrive summing them in order (`lk_gemm_blk`'s `tail`, `split`, `part`, `cnt`). A wide chunk's `f32` router cuts each tile of its logits GEMM along `k` the same way, enough parts to give each multiprocessor two blocks (`kernels::gemm_f32_splits`, `Launch::Moe::router_split`). `lk_head_tiled` takes 128 rows a block (`HEAD_TILE_M`), a thread 8 rows by 4 outputs, its results unchanged bit for bit. `lk_attention_tiled` spreads a warp's lanes over groups of queries and of keys (and of values), fetches each chunk's queries and keys and each group's values a step ahead, its results unchanged bit for bit. Over DeepSeek-V2-Lite's 512-token chunk the dense GEMMs drop by 6–14%, the router from 143 µs a layer to 44, the latent expansion from 296 µs a half to 223 and the attention from 593 µs to 557, and the prompt prefills at 2,994 tokens a second, up from 2,786 (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **The latent attention's decode step reads each cached row once.** Under the quantized numerics, an attention below the tiles whose values are its key rows' leading part, every query head reading the one key/value group (DeepSeek's latent attention absorbed, in the decode step and the narrow chunks), now runs every head in one block (`kernels::ATTENTION_SHARED`'s `lk_attention_shared`, `Kernel::AttentionShared`, `Launch::Attention::shared`): a row's span is cut into the decode step's chunks in every form, each a whole number of 16-row steps (`kernels::attn_shared_splits`, `ATTN_SHARED_STEP`), a block a chunk, which holds its heads' queries in registers, stages each step's rows in shared memory by `cp.async` while the last step multiplies, scores four heads against eight keys a warp with each lane's sums exchanged to one score a lane, and weighs every head's values from the same rows; a launch of its own folds the chunks, a block a head and 128 values (`lk_attention_fold`, `ATTN_FOLD_PAIRS`). The choice reads the values as the key rows' own part whether the attention reads them in place or, with `strided_values` held back, a launch slices them out first, and the kernel then reads the sliced rows (`VK`): the same values, so the program apart computes the fused program's results bit for bit. Where heads took blocks of their own, each read the whole span. `lk_attention`'s last block folds the chunks a value pair a thread, every head's pair of a chunk read at once, its results unchanged bit for bit. On DeepSeek-V2-Lite the decode step's attention drops from 28.2 µs a layer to 15.8 after a 512-token prompt, and the step decodes at 142.8 tokens a second, up from 136.6 (`lane_compile_lock`, `cuda_lane_lock`).
- **The decode step's expert down projection four outputs a block.** `lk_moe_down` takes `W` consecutive outputs a block (`kernels::moe_down_rows`): each kept expert's warp dots its `W` rows with one load of the hidden row, and the shared expert's rows take a warp each where they are wider than an expert's, so no warp holds its block back; every output's dots and sums are those of a block of one output, bit for bit. A Q8_0 matrix's rows dot together through `lk_q8_rows_dot` (each row's sums in `lk_q8_row_dot`'s order, the rows' weight loads in flight over one load of each activation word), which `lk_gemv_dot` now takes for several Q8_0 rows a warp. On the 3060 the down projection at DeepSeek-V2-Lite's widths drops from 84.4 µs to 72.6 (Q8_0 beside a shared Q6_K, 97% of a plain read of its bytes) and from 77.6 µs to 54.5 (Q5_0 beside a shared Q4_K, two-word numerics), gpt-oss-20b's MXFP4 from 150.8 µs to 96.0 and a Qwen3-30B-A3B shape's Q4_K from 46.3 µs to 31.0 (`cuda_lane_lock`).
- **Dense Q8_0 prefill on the block GEMM; a chunk's conv and `f32` projections across its rows.** A wide chunk's Q8_0 projections run on `lk_gemm_blk<8>` — its split arrays through the block GEMM and its last-wave split, as every block format's — where `lk_gemm_q8` (now gone) took them: bit for bit the same under the one-word numerics, within 1.7e-6 of the largest output under the two-word numerics, and 1.3–1.9× faster (Qwen3-8B's 12288 × 4096 gate from 2.81 ms to 1.51 at 512 rows).
